# ARGUS REDLINE v0.7.0 Physical Qualification Results

**Milestone:** v0.7.0 — Node-Originated Events and Reliable Delivery
**Qualification status:** PASS — Stage 16 bench and Stage 17 physical matrix complete with recorded fixture limitations
**Current gate:** Stage 18 — Documentation and Release Closeout
**Released firmware baseline:** v0.6.0
**Qualification firmware:** v0.7.0 behavior physically qualified while firmware metadata intentionally remained v0.6.0; promoted to v0.7.0 only in Stage 18

## 1. Qualification Scope

This document records the physical bench integration and qualification evidence
for ARGUS REDLINE v0.7.0.

The normative qualification authority is:

`docs/V0.7.0_IMPLEMENTATION_BRIEF.md`

Stages 1–15, the repaired-candidate Stage 16 bench gate, and the Stage 17
physical matrix are complete. Selectively uninducible fault boundaries are
recorded as physical-fixture limitations and retain deterministic/native fault
injection as their authority.

Failures, aborted runs, limitations, and remediation attempts are preserved
chronologically rather than rewritten after later success.

No version promotion, tag, release, merge, push, or publication is implied by
this document.

```text
port=/dev/ttyACM0
```

No RX frame was reported by the Host utility. No retry or subsequent Host

no bytes is not distinguished by this observation. No physical boot/OLED
observation was collected before the stop. The owning layer is not yet proven;
the evidence bounds the deviation to Hub boot/runtime readiness, Host service,
or the USB CDC/host delivery boundary rather than the immutable Host request
encoding itself. Firmware metadata was not changed and remains intentionally

### 6.2 Post-failure boundary-isolation diagnostic — 2026-09-06

This was a diagnostic investigation only. Stage 16 was not resumed, the
original Section 6.1 failure remains authoritative, and no firmware, protocol,
dependency, configuration, qualification-semantic, or flash change occurred.

#### Step A — post-failure physical identity

At `2026-09-06T02:09:54-05:00`, read-only udev discovery reported:

| Role | Port | USB serial / MAC | VID:PID | udev initialization ID |
|---|---|---|---|---:|
| Hub | `/dev/ttyACM0` | `F8:5B:1B:A2:A0:E4` | `303A:1001` | `2728586425` |
| Node | `/dev/ttyACM1` | `F8:5B:1B:A1:0A:E0` | `303A:1001` | `2737530481` |

Both identities, ports, and udev initialization IDs matched the earlier
enumeration. Neither device had disappeared or re-enumerated after the initial
HELLO timeout.

#### Step B — Hub boot and Host readiness inspection

Production Hub source calls `Serial.begin(115200)` first in `setup()`, followed
by `Serial.setTxTimeoutMs(0)` and disabled debug output. Host RX is not serviced
until `setup()` completes and `runtimeState.isReady()` is true. Before that
point, initialization performs 140 ms of explicit delay, OLED initialization,
settings/NVS loading, persistent Hub Event-ledger recovery, SPI initialization,
and `radio.begin(915.0)`. These OLED, NVS, and radio operations can extend the
readiness interval. A radio initialization failure leaves runtime not ready and
therefore leaves Host RX gated.

The production machine-stream build intentionally emits no unsolicited Host
bytes; version/radio prints are limited to text-stream or characterization
builds. An immediately issued HELLO can therefore arrive before foreground
Host service begins. However, source contains no intentional multi-second boot
delay, and the fixed 140 ms delay alone does not explain the original 10-second
timeout. Normal buffering would also be expected to preserve already received
Host bytes until the loop services them. Immediate post-flash readiness was a
plausible variable but was not sufficient by source inspection alone to assign
the failure.

#### Step C — passive safe-open check

The first local passive-check command had a Python quoting syntax error. It
exited before importing or opening the serial device, transmitted no bytes,
and produced no hardware evidence. This procedural failure was corrected
without changing the intended diagnostic.

The corrected check directly reused
`tools.host_reference.redline_host.open_serial_transport()`. It constructed the
serial object closed, disabled software/hardware flow control, set DTR and RTS
inactive before opening, transmitted no Host bytes, observed for approximately
three seconds, and closed the stream. Open succeeded and pyserial reported DTR
and RTS false both immediately after open and immediately before close.

Contrary to the intended passive behavior, the read returned 265 bytes of an
ESP-ROM boot banner. The decisive decoded banner fields were:

```text
ESP-ROM:esp32s3-20210327
rst:0x15 (USB_UART_CHIP_RESET),boot:0x9 (SPI_FAST_FLASH_BOOT)
Saved PC:0x403780fe
entry 0x403c88b8
```

Observed check summary:

```text
open_succeeded=1
dtr=0
rts=0
elapsed_seconds=3.021252
received_bytes=265
preclose_dtr=0
preclose_rts=0
closed=1
```

This is direct evidence that opening the Hub USB CDC device caused or coincided
with an ESP32-S3 USB-UART chip reset despite the Host utility preserving
inactive DTR/RTS state. Production intentionally emits no unsolicited machine
frames, so these ROM bytes are reset evidence rather than expected Host output.

A read-only udev check immediately afterward at
`2026-09-06T02:10:49-05:00` still reported the Hub at `/dev/ttyACM0` with USB
serial/MAC `F8:5B:1B:A2:A0:E4` and the same udev initialization ID
`2728586425`; the Node was likewise unchanged. Thus no Linux tty disappearance,
port change, or udev re-enumeration was observed, although the MCU reset is
proven by its ROM banner.

#### Step D — controlled HELLO retry

**NOT EXECUTED.** The unexpected MCU reset during safe Host access triggered
the mandatory diagnostic stop. No Host request, malformed traffic, Event
operation, Stage-17 scenario, or additional serial open followed.

#### Diagnostic disposition

**STOPPED — unexpected MCU reset during the passive safe-open check.** The
smallest proven boundary is below REDLINE Host request parsing: no application
bytes were transmitted, yet opening Linux `cdc_acm` produced an ESP-ROM
`USB_UART_CHIP_RESET`. This makes the USB CDC/host-driver/open boundary the
leading owner and provides a concrete mechanism capable of explaining the
initial HELLO loss: the first request may have been written while the MCU was
resetting or before USB/firmware Host service recovered. That causal sequence
has not been directly proven because the mandated stop prevented the controlled
retry.

Historical v0.6 qualification investigated a different symptom in the older
Arduino framework: application TX was accepted by HWCDC but not delivered to
the PC. The current evidence instead shows an open-associated chip reset before
any Host request, despite the current Arduino-ESP32 3.3.9 framework and the
safe-open control-line state. The earlier framework migration therefore does
not dispose this Linux open/reset observation.

The next smallest experiment should be a separately authorized, read-only
host/USB control-path capture around exactly one passive safe open (kernel USB
and `cdc_acm` events plus USB control requests), with no Host payload. Its goal
would be to determine which open-time control request causes
`USB_UART_CHIP_RESET` and whether Linux driver behavior differs from the
pyserial-visible DTR/RTS state. A controlled HELLO retry should not occur until
that reset behavior is understood or an approved reset-aware readiness
procedure is defined.

The source utility's SHA-256 is
`0d2c3b6c8a2e61d03dd4adaf67572ddfdc044eee819f7c9424dccd90d909b157`.
The inspected installed POSIX backend is
`/home/phillipfox/.platformio/penv/lib/python3.14/site-packages/serial/serialposix.py`,

`5d56f98513391e1766a1847a04e2e1202ced4172a152803ba1c823a149a9b6f9`.

#### Exact open ordering and competing mechanism

`redline_host.py:393` constructs `serial.Serial()` closed, sets baud/read/write
timeouts, disables `xonxoff`, `rtscts`, and `dsrdtr`, sets `dtr=False` and
`rts=False`, assigns the port, and calls `.open()` exactly once. These property
assignments do not issue hardware requests while the object is closed.

The installed POSIX `.open()` implementation then performs this sequence:

1. `os.open(port, O_RDWR | O_NOCTTY | O_NONBLOCK)`;
2. `tcgetattr` and `tcsetattr(TCSANOW)` to configure raw serial operation;
3. a separate `TIOCMBIC` clearing `TIOCM_DTR`;
4. a separate `TIOCMBIC` clearing `TIOCM_RTS`;
5. `tcflush(TCIFLUSH)` and pipe setup, then marks the Python object open.

The `dtr` and `rts` getters return stored Python state, not a measurement of all
previous USB transfers. Also, the POSIX backend tolerates EINVAL/ENOTTY from
control updates; neither failure is known to have occurred here. It does not
explicitly modify HUPCL, so its actual inherited value needs trace evidence.
The previous statement that DTR/RTS remained false establishes logical API
state at sampled times, not absence of intermediate bus-level assertions.

External upstream Linux source shows nonblocking tty opening can raise both
lines when the existing baud is nonzero; the ACM driver translates raising
both to a CDC `SET_CONTROL_LINE_STATE` request. This is source comparison, not
an observed execution trace of Kali's exact patched 7.1.5 binary.
[TTY source](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/master/drivers/tty/tty_port.c),
[ACM source, v7.1](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v7.1/drivers/usb/class/cdc-acm.c).

The strongest predicted USB sequence is therefore:

| Predicted point | CDC bitmap | DTR | RTS | Consequence to test |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Kernel tty open asserts both | `0x0003` | 1 | 1 | Not visible in the cached false properties |
| pyserial clears DTR first | `0x0002` | 0 | 1 | Candidate USB Serial/JTAG reset-triggering intermediate state |
| pyserial clears RTS second | `0x0000` | 0 | 0 | Both logical and bus state finally inactive |

**These three transfers have NOT been captured.** The installed kernel header
`include/uapi/linux/usb/cdc.h` confirms `SET_CONTROL_LINE_STATE=0x22`,
`SET_LINE_CODING=0x20`, DTR bit 0 and RTS bit 1. pyserial explicitly documents
that OS/driver activation during open can produce a control-line glitch even
with inactive requested states.
[pyserial open documentation](https://pyserial.readthedocs.io/en/latest/pyserial_api.html#serial.Serial.open).

#### ESP32-S3 meaning and historical comparison

The repository board definition `boards/heltec_wifi_lora_32_V4.json` sets
`ARDUINO_USB_MODE=1` and `ARDUINO_USB_CDC_ON_BOOT=1`. Together with the installed
`HardwareSerial.h`, these select `HWCDC` over the built-in USB Serial/JTAG
peripheral. The repository variant supplies VID/PID and GPIO assignments; it
does not implement the USB reset handshake. This is distinct from a
programmable TinyUSB CDC stack or an external USB-UART adapter.

The actual bundled ESP32-S3 ROM and SoC headers define reason 21 (`0x15`) as
`USB_UART_CHIP_RESET` / `RESET_REASON_CORE_USB_UART`: the USB UART path reset the
digital core. Despite the ROM label's word CHIP, the header describes a core
reset. Separate reasons exist for USB JTAG (`0x16`), software, watchdog, power,
and brownout resets. A USB-UART core reset is not itself proof of a USB bus
reset, an external EN pulse, or a tty re-enumeration. Inspected `HWCDC.cpp`
handles a USB BUS_RESET interrupt by updating transport state; that handler
does not call a software MCU restart.

Espressif's ESP32-S3 TRM, section 33.4.2 and tables 33.4-3/33.4-4, documents the
virtual RTS/DTR reset mechanism, including the RTS=1/DTR=0 reset state. It also
describes Windows drivers that defer propagating DTR until RTS is set. That
provides a plausible reason separate POSIX clears could behave differently
from the previously successful Windows safe-open sequence; it does not prove
which transfers the historical Windows machine sent.
[Espressif TRM](https://documentation.espressif.com/esp32-s3_technical_reference_manual_en.pdf).

Historical Section 21 records genuine Windows open/reset remediation; Section
26 records the separate HWCDC accepted-write-but-no-delivery issue. Both are
relevant precedents, but neither supplies this Linux trace. No claim is made
that all three failures share one mechanism.

The earlier 265-byte ROM banner reports the reset reason of the boot producing
those bytes. Its observation alone did not timestamp the triggering reset or
exclude previously buffered boot output. The proposed trace must correlate a
fresh USB control sequence with the first received ROM bytes before asserting
open-time causality. This qualification of the earlier interpretation does not
erase its observation or the original FAIL.

#### Preliminary hypothesis ranking — before the trace

| Hypothesis | Supporting evidence | Contrary evidence / unknown | Confidence | Smallest discriminator |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Kernel assertion followed by pyserial's separate DTR-then-RTS clears | Exact installed Python order; upstream tty/ACM behavior; documented S3 reset state | Actual Kali USB sequence and initial line state uncaptured | Leading, medium-high; unproven physically | Correlate `0x22` values with open and TIOCMBIC syscalls in the proposed single capture |
| Earlier buffered reset output or close-time reset misattributed to open | Prior capture had no per-chunk USB/syscall timing; uploader deliberately reset the board | Passive check's input flush makes some stale-buffer explanations less likely, but device buffering remains unmeasured | Material timing alternative | Timestamp ROM USB IN completion against open/close/control requests in the same capture |
| Other process or automatic probing | MM candidate tag is present | MM inactive, no visible holders/probe process or matching custom rule | Low, not excluded by snapshots | Privileged holder snapshots during the same capture; unexplained requests remain unattributed without system-wide tracing |
| USB bus reset, rebind, or autosuspend | Could cause an access-time disturbance in principle | No corresponding kernel/udev events; boards forced on; suspended time zero; reason identifies USB UART core reset | Low | Concurrent kernel/udev events and control capture |
| REDLINE/framework restart, watchdog, external reset circuit | Boot/runtime readiness was previously unobserved | Payload-free access, hardware USB personality, distinct reason code, no restart in inspected HWCDC bus-reset handler | Low | If reset occurs without predicted controls, retain trace and reassess this layer before any firmware experiment |

#### Proposed single bounded trace — NOT EXECUTED

The experiment will use the existing safe helper unchanged for exactly one
Hub open, three seconds of passive reads, and one normal close. It will send
zero application payload bytes: no `write()` to the tty, no HELLO, and no Event
request. The Node will not be opened. It will retain the original control-line
and flow-control ordering, with no extra termios mutation or retry.

Planned observation commands/tools:

1. With explicit permission, `sudo modprobe usbmon` loads only the already
   installed observation module. No package install, driver unload/rebind,
   kernel configuration edit, or udev change is proposed. Module unloading
   afterward also requires permission and will not be done implicitly.
2. Start a maximum 20-second capture through the existing tcpdump with sudo:
   `sudo timeout --signal=INT 20 tcpdump -i usbmon1 -s 512 -U -w <trace-dir>/hub-usb.pcap 'link[11] = 7'`.
   Confirm the USB_LINUX/MMAPPED header and device-address filter before the
   serial open; save only current Hub-address traffic. If the interface/filter
   cannot be verified, abort before opening. Rediscover identity immediately
   before the experiment and stop if it differs from the planned target.
3. Concurrent `journalctl -k -f -n 0 -o short-unix` and
   `udevadm monitor --kernel --udev --property --subsystem-match=usb --subsystem-match=tty`
   observe reset/disconnect/bind events. Both observers receive a bounded
   lifetime and are stopped after capture. Record pre/post udev identities and
   root-visible tty holder snapshots without opening the devices.
4. Use the installed GDB on the diagnostic Python child process, not on the
   ESP32: `gdb -q -nx -batch -x <trace-dir>/syscalls.gdb --args
   /home/phillipfox/.platformio/penv/bin/python -B <trace-dir>/passive_open.py`.
   A narrowly filtered `catch syscall openat ioctl read write close` trace will
   retain entry/return timestamps, tty fd, ioctl numbers/arguments, termios
   state including HUPCL, return values, and received bytes. Disable debuginfod
   and automatic symbol downloads. GDB's Python support was verified without
   starting any serial access; child tracing needs no sudo under the observed
   `ptrace_scope=0`. Validate tracing with a no-hardware fixture before the one
   authorized open.
5. The passive helper timestamps just before/after the existing
   `open_serial_transport('/dev/ttyACM0', 115200, 0.05, 3.0)`, each nonempty
   read, and close, using realtime plus monotonic nanoseconds. It uses
   `try/finally` for one close, calls no serial write method, and preserves raw
   bytes. Temporary diagnostic files belong in a fresh `mktemp -d` directory;
   production source and installed pyserial remain unchanged.
6. Decode the saved capture offline with tshark: correlate USB `0x22` control
   bitmaps, `0x20` line coding (if any), other requests, control completion
   status, bulk IN ROM data, and absence/presence of bulk OUT. Report actual
   observed values, not the predicted `3 -> 2 -> 0` sequence unless captured.

Limitations: GDB syscall stops add latency and can widen intermediate line
states; a success under tracing would not prove normal untraced safety. usbmon
records host URB requests/completions, not a physical USB wire analyzer. A
fixed Hub-address filter cannot follow a new address after re-enumeration;
kernel/udev observations must flag that event. Holder snapshots cannot exclude
every short-lived competing access. A normal close may itself generate kernel
HUPCL control traffic; capture must continue through close. No additional open
will occur if tooling fails after the single open is attempted.

#### Interim next action

Request approval for loading the existing usbmon module and privileged,
Hub-filtered capture around the one specified passive open. This is the single
recommended next experiment. Do not install strace or replace the trace with
another unobserved open.

If the predicted intermediate `0x0002` state is physically confirmed, the
narrow proposed remediation would belong in the Host-side POSIX opening
adapter: evaluate applying both inactive modem-control bits atomically after
kernel open, without changing installed pyserial or firmware. That is only a
conditional proposal; ordering, disconnect behavior, and Windows compatibility
would need a separately authorized focused verification. Merely adding a
post-open sleep would hide the reset without preserving device state, and
clearing HUPCL alone would not remove a kernel assertion during open.

No firmware modification is currently indicated. Stage 16 remains blocked
pending physical causal evidence and an approved disposition of the reset.

### 6.5 Stage 16.1 USB CDC Reset Investigation — completed one-shot trace

**Capture date/time:** 2026-09-06, `02:35:07.985821`–`02:35:11.765331-05:00`
(`07:35:07.985821`–`07:35:11.765331Z`), approximately 3.780 seconds of
USB-capture process lifetime, within the authorized maximum of 20 seconds.
**Offline analysis date:** 2026-09-06.
The operator completed the previously authorized launcher with local sudo
authentication and explicitly instructed that neither tty be opened again.
This section analyzes those preserved files only. No additional tty open,
Host request, rebuild, reflash, or privileged system change was performed
during analysis. Sections 6.1–6.4 retain their original chronological findings.

**Disposition: A — Host-side opening-sequence issue identified.** The captured
Linux TTY assertion followed by pyserial's separate DTR-first/RTS-second clears
sent the ESP32-S3's documented USB Serial/JTAG reset-triggering state. This
establishes the mechanism for this diagnostic reset with high confidence;
remediation is proposed below, not implemented or verified. It does not turn
the original Stage-16.1 failure into a pass.

#### Environment, identities, and observation integrity

- Source branch remains `v0.7.0-physical-qualification`, HEAD
  `9e2622009dd2912ea38a4b841c1293f94603a177`. Tracked files remain clean; the only
  `git status --short` entry is this intentionally untracked evidence document.
  Stage-15 candidate/artifact identities remain those recorded in Sections 2–3;
  no artifact was replaced or flashed in this phase.
- Host environment is the Section 6.3 Dreadnought/Kali inventory:
  `7.1.5+kali-amd64`, in-tree `cdc_acm`, PlatformIO Core 6.1.19,
  Python 3.14.6, pyserial 3.5. No package or kernel configuration was changed.
- Hub identity was verified before capture, immediately before opening, and
  after closing: `/dev/ttyACM0`, `F8:5B:1B:A2:A0:E4`, `303A:1001`,
  `cdc_acm`, USB path `1-6.3`, bus 1 address 7.
  `USEC_INITIALIZED=2728586425` remained unchanged.
- Node identity remained `/dev/ttyACM1`, `F8:5B:1B:A1:0A:E0`,
  `303A:1001`, `cdc_acm`, path `1-6.4`, initialization ID `2737530481`.
  The launcher did not open the Node.
- Privileged `fuser -v` snapshots before/after returned exit 1 with empty
  stdout/stderr: no holders found. These snapshots are not continuous
  system-wide process tracing.
- The authorized `modprobe usbmon` succeeded, exit 0. The launcher used
  `tcpdump 4.99.6`/libpcap 1.10.6, `usbmon1`, `USB_LINUX_MMAPPED`, snapshot
  length 512, and the checked Hub-only filter `link[11] = 7`.
  tcpdump recorded **74 packets, 74 received by filter, 0 kernel drops**.
  All 74 saved records are fully captured and address bus 1 device 7.
- GDB 17.2 observed the existing Python helper as operator UID 1000,
  PID 66184, not as root. The traced process exited normally, exit 0;
  tcpdump exited 0. The kernel and udev observers were stopped deliberately
  by SIGINT after completion, return code -2, not by a capture failure.
  `journalctl -k -f -n 0 -o short-unix` produced no messages or errors.
  `udevadm monitor --kernel --udev --property` for USB/tty produced only its
  startup notice, no events or errors.
- The unchanged `open_serial_transport` was invoked exactly once at
  115200 baud, read timeout 0.05 s, write timeout 3 s, then passively read for
  approximately three seconds and closed normally. Construction was closed;
  `xonxoff`, `rtscts`, `dsrdtr`, DTR and RTS were false before `.open()`.
  Sampled Python DTR/RTS properties were also false after open and before close.
  The code contains no serial write. The trace records one tty `openat`,
  eight ioctls, ten reads, one close, and **zero tty writes**. USB capture
  independently contains **no bulk OUT transfer**: zero application payload.
- `usbmon` was left loaded by the authorized launcher. It was not unloaded
  or otherwise reconfigured during this analysis.

#### Correlated syscall and USB control evidence

All times in this table are `2026-09-06 07:35:SS` UTC. USB timestamps are
usbmon host-side URB submission/completion times; syscall bounds are GDB
recorder timestamps, not a hardware measurement of the CPU's reset edge.
GDB changes scheduling, so the measured gaps are diagnostic-run timings,
not uninstrumented timing guarantees.

| Time, seconds | Directly recorded observation | Interpretation |
|---|---|---|
| `08.123090`–`08.123363` | `openat("/dev/ttyACM0", O_RDWR\|O_NOCTTY\|O_NONBLOCK\|O_CLOEXEC)` returns fd 3 | Kernel device open succeeds |
| `08.123227`, PCAP frame 26 | `SET_CONTROL_LINE_STATE`, `wValue=0x0003`; completion frame 29 at `08.123295`, status 0 | Both DTR and RTS asserted during `openat`, before userspace modem-control ioctls |
| `08.123466`–`08.124211` | Four successful `TCGETS2` and one `TCSETS2`; `c_cflag=0x1cb2`, HUPCL set throughout, input/output speed 115200 | Existing raw termios state reapplied; no HUPCL toggle observed |
| `08.124295`–`08.124444` | `ioctl(fd=3, TIOCMBIC, mask=0x00000002)` returns 0 | pyserial clears `TIOCM_DTR` first |
| `08.124316`, frame 30 | `SET_CONTROL_LINE_STATE`, `wValue=0x0002`; completion frame 31 at `08.124384`, status 0 | DTR=0, RTS=1: documented USB Serial/JTAG reset-triggering state |
| `08.124529`–`08.124673` | `ioctl(fd=3, TIOCMBIC, mask=0x00000004)` returns 0 | pyserial clears `TIOCM_RTS` second |
| `08.124550`, frame 32 | `SET_CONTROL_LINE_STATE`, `wValue=0x0000`; completion frame 33 at `08.124616`, status 0 | Both inactive; reset-release state |
| `08.124754`–`08.124823` | `ioctl(fd=3, TCFLSH, TCIFLUSH)` returns 0 | Existing helper/backend input flush occurs before received ROM bytes |
| `08.124910` | Helper reports `.open()` returned; DTR/RTS and all three flow-control flags false | Final cached properties do not expose preceding transient bus states |
| `08.125748`, frame 34 | First nonempty bulk IN: 26 bytes, `ESP-ROM:esp32s3-20210327\r\n` | ROM output begins 1.432 ms after frame 30, after Python open returned |
| `08.126928`, frame 38 | Bulk IN contains `rst:0x15 (USB_UART_CHIP_RESET),boot:0x9 (SPI_FAST_FLASH_BOOT)` plus CRLF | Reset reason independently visible in bus capture and process reads |
| `11.159138`–`11.197863` | Normal `close(fd=3)` returns 0 | Single passive open ends |
| `11.159232`, frame 56 | `SET_CONTROL_LINE_STATE`, `wValue=0x0000`; completion frame 57 at `11.159345`, status 0 | Close sends both inactive together; no `0x0002` state at close |

The exact eight-byte USB setup packets, in chronological order, are:

```text
frame 26: 21 22 03 00 00 00 00 00
frame 30: 21 22 02 00 00 00 00 00
frame 32: 21 22 00 00 00 00 00 00
frame 56: 21 22 00 00 00 00 00 00
```

Each has `bmRequestType=0x21`, request `0x22`, interface index 0 and data
length 0: ordinary CDC ACM control requests, not Host Protocol payload.
**USB bitmap bits are DTR=0x01, RTS=0x02; Linux ioctl masks are instead
TIOCM_DTR=0x02, TIOCM_RTS=0x04.** The intermediate USB value `0x0002` is RTS
alone, not DTR alone. Frame 32 follows frame 30 by 234 microseconds; this is
the interval between host submissions, not a measured physical reset duration.

Only three other control submissions were captured, all before tty open:
standard GET_DESCRIPTOR requests for the device descriptor and configuration
descriptor (9-byte header then 98-byte full descriptor), frames 1/3/5.
These are not a USB reset or Host traffic. Capture-tool descriptor discovery
is a plausible origin, but the PCAP does not identify their initiating PID.
There was **no SET_LINE_CODING (`0x20`), SEND_BREAK, vendor reset request, or
JTAG endpoint traffic** in the Hub capture. No 1200-baud change was observed.
Frames 58–74 complete pending IN URBs with `-ENOENT` during normal close,
after the ROM output, consistent with driver cancellation rather than a
preceding disconnect or transfer failure. The four CDC control requests all
completed successfully.

#### ROM output and reset meaning

The 265 bytes in successful bulk IN completions, the concatenated GDB-observed
read results, and the helper's complete received buffer match byte-for-byte.
SHA-256 of those bytes (including CRLF endings):
`a0572e41933fd6a9028de5cc706f58c8d74a337791c84c6d8f9a29c17b3f2b9c`.

```text
ESP-ROM:esp32s3-20210327
Build:Mar 27 2021
rst:0x15 (USB_UART_CHIP_RESET),boot:0x9 (SPI_FAST_FLASH_BOOT)
Saved PC:0x403780fe
SPIWP:0xee
mode:DIO, clock div:1
load:0x3fce2820,len:0x10cc
load:0x403c8700,len:0xc2c
load:0x403cb700,len:0x30c0
entry 0x403c88b8
```

The Section 6.3 local source inspection identifies this board configuration
as ESP32-S3 fixed-function USB Serial/JTAG / Arduino `HWCDC`. Its bundled
reset definitions identify `0x15` as a USB UART digital-core reset, distinct
from USB JTAG `0x16`, watchdog, software restart, brownout or power reset.
This matches a USB control-line-triggered core reset while USB identity and
the tty remain intact; it does not require an external EN pulse or a
host-initiated USB bus reset.

External documentation supplies the hardware interpretation, not the physical
observations: Espressif's ESP32-S3 TRM section 33.4.2, tables 33.4-3/33.4-4,
identifies clearing DTR while RTS remains asserted as a reset action and
clearing RTS as release. The captured `3 -> 2 -> 0` sequence and immediately
following ROM output match that mechanism.
[Espressif TRM](https://documentation.espressif.com/esp32-s3_technical_reference_manual_en.pdf).
Upstream Linux ACM source maps its TTY DTR/RTS callback to assertion of both
bits and modem-control clears to CDC requests; the observed first request
falls inside `openat`, and the next two inside the corresponding pyserial
ioctls. No kernel function tracing was performed, so source-level attribution
is corroboration rather than a trace of Kali's exact patched kernel functions.
[Linux ACM source, v7.1](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v7.1/drivers/usb/class/cdc-acm.c).
pyserial's warning about OS/driver assertions on open explains why setting
closed-object properties false is insufficient to guarantee no bus transient.
[pyserial open documentation](https://pyserial.readthedocs.io/en/latest/pyserial_api.html#serial.Serial.open).

The reset-triggering requests occurred after OS `openat` returned but during
pyserial `.open()`. The first received ROM bytes occurred after pyserial
`.open()` returned. The MCU's exact internal reset-assertion instant was not
measured. Unlike the earlier banner-only evidence, this capture directly
establishes a reset-triggering command immediately preceding ROM output;
the pre-sequence IN completions contained zero bytes.

#### Hypothesis ranking and limits

| Hypothesis | Supporting evidence | Contrary evidence / limit | Confidence and smallest discriminating test |
|---|---|---|---|
| Linux TTY assertion plus pyserial DTR-first clearing invokes USB Serial/JTAG reset | Actual `3 -> 2 -> 0` requests align with open/ioctl boundaries; successful control completions; immediate `0x15` ROM output | One GDB-instrumented run; an avoiding sequence has not been tested | HIGH, mechanism identified for this run. Separately authorize the single atomic-clear verification below |
| Termios/HUPCL or baud-rate touch is the primary reset cause | Termios is reapplied; HUPCL was enabled | HUPCL never toggles; speeds stay 115200; no SET_LINE_CODING; close sends only 0, after reset | LOW as an independent cause. Keep termios unchanged in the proposed verification to isolate modem-control sequencing |
| Another process probes or resets the tty | Generic ModemManager-candidate tag exists; snapshots cannot exclude every transient process | ModemManager inactive in prior inspection; privileged holder snapshots empty; all reset-relevant requests align with traced ioctls; no unexplained OUT payload | LOW. Only if a reset recurs without the traced sequence would system-wide tty-open attribution be warranted |
| USB bus reset, rebind, disconnect, or autosuspend causes the reset | Such host events were initially plausible; device-only filtering has limits | No kernel/udev events; same address/path/identity/initialization IDs; prior power state active, autosuspend disabled; direct reset handshake explains output | VERY LOW on current evidence. Only if the focused workaround fails, a parent-hub-inclusive bus/event trace would discriminate this alternative |
| REDLINE/Arduino application restart, external EN circuit, or historical HWCDC write-loss defect | Firmware is involved after boot; application Host readiness remains untested | Native USB control trigger and `0x15` reason account for reset without these causes; zero application writes; distinct from historical accepted-write loss | VERY LOW as cause of this reset. First remove the observed trigger in the focused Host-side test; firmware experimentation is not warranted now |

No device disappearance, logical re-enumeration or interface unbind/rebind was
recorded. **LIMITATION:** usbmon records host URBs, not electrical USB reset
signaling. Filtering bus 1 address 7 excludes parent-hub requests, address 0
enumeration traffic, other devices and other buses. Empty kernel/udev logs and
stable initialization IDs support continuity but do not prove absence of every
possible bus-level event. PID attribution is limited to the traced process and
the before/after holder snapshots. No EN waveform, firmware uptime, OLED
observation, post-boot Host response or radio readiness was measured.

The historical Windows safe-open success is not contradicted: the same
closed-object state assignments can produce different driver-level transfers.
Espressif documents Windows CDC implementations that defer DTR propagation
until an RTS operation. No historical Windows USB trace is available here,
so its exact bus sequence is not claimed. The separate v0.6 HWCDC write-loss
failure is not reproduced by this zero-payload experiment.

The original request `0x1601` remains **FAIL / timeout**, with no diagnostic
HELLO retry executed. Opening-induced reboot is now a strong explanation for
why a promptly written HELLO could encounter a rebooting/not-ready Hub, even
long after flashing. However, this trace did not capture that request, its
delivery, or HostTransport/parser state: the precise cause of its missing
response remains inferred, not independently proven. Sleeping before opening
does not remove this observed open-induced trigger; sleeping afterward would
not preserve the runtime state lost to reset.

#### Proposed next action — authorization required; NOT EXECUTED

Choose **A: Host-side procedural/open-adapter issue identified**, with the
underlying interaction explicitly spanning Linux TTY/CDC assertion, pyserial
POSIX ordering and the ESP32-S3 hardware reset handshake. This is not evidence
that REDLINE firmware or the kernel needs a patch.

The narrow proposed change belongs in a Linux-specific opening adapter used
by `tools/host_reference/redline_host.py`: while retaining closed construction,
all three flow controls disabled, DTR/RTS false before opening, and exactly one
open, apply **both inactive bits together** with
`TIOCMBIC(TIOCM_DTR | TIOCM_RTS)` (mask `0x06`) at the first post-kernel-open
modem-control update, before any separate DTR-only clear. Do not patch the
installed pyserial package or change the Windows path. Merely reversing the
closed-object property assignments does not change POSIX `.open()` ordering.
The proposal must account for error/close behavior and private backend-hook
compatibility; it is not yet an approved implementation or proven workaround.

**One focused future verification:** after separate authorization of a scoped
adapter experiment, perform one equivalently bounded passive Hub open/close
under the same observations, with termios and all other settings unchanged.
Require no application payload; capture both-bit deassertion directly
(`0x0003 -> 0x0000`, allowing redundant zero requests), no intermediate RTS-only
`0x0002`, no fresh reset banner, and stable identity/enumeration through close.
Any unexpected transition/reset is a failure and stop. Absence of a banner
alone is not sufficient; the USB control sequence is the primary acceptance
evidence. That test has not been performed and would not itself qualify Host
Protocol behavior or authorize restarting Stage 16.

No firmware modification, reflash, dependency change, workstation rule change,
or qualification-semantics change appears necessary to test this explanation.
**Stage 16 remains FAIL / STOPPED and blocked pending an authorized, validated
Host-side disposition. Stage 17 has not begun.**

### 6.6 Linux Host atomic-open implementation and non-physical validation

**Date/time:** 2026-09-06, approximately `02:47`–`02:57-05:00`.
**Status:** IMPLEMENTED / NON-PHYSICAL VALIDATION PASS; physical workaround
verification not yet executed. Stage 16 remains FAIL / STOPPED.

The smallest scoped change was made in
`tools/host_reference/redline_host.py`. On Linux only, the default serial
factory now subclasses the installed pyserial backend and intercepts the first
inactive modem-control update during each `.open()`. It issues one
`TIOCMBIC(TIOCM_DTR | TIOCM_RTS)` with native ioctl mask `0x06`, then permits
pyserial's second update to run normally as a harmless redundant clear. The
existing construction and policy remain: object constructed closed,
`xonxoff=False`, `rtscts=False`, `dsrdtr=False`, DTR false and RTS false before
one `.open()`. There is no sleep, baud/termios change, protocol change,
firmware change, dependency change, or installed-pyserial patch.

The adapter is active only while pyserial `.open()` executes; a `finally`
clears its pending flag, so later explicit control-line changes retain normal
pyserial behavior. The captured pyserial 3.5 implementation exposes no public
hook between its underlying Linux `os.open()` and separate DTR/RTS updates, so
the protected `_update_dtr_state` and `_update_rts_state` hooks are the
narrowest viable integration point. Compatibility risk is bounded as follows:

- both hooks are checked for callability before any tty is opened;
- a backend without them fails locally before device access;
- either hook order is safe because whichever inactive update occurs first
  performs the combined clear;
- an atomic ioctl `OSError`, including one pyserial would otherwise suppress,
  is converted to `RuntimeError`, causing pyserial's existing open-error cleanup
  to close the fd rather than return with asserted lines;
- non-Linux platforms return the original `serial.Serial` class unchanged;
- explicitly injected serial factories retain the pre-existing behavior used
  by tests and callers.

Focused tests were added to
`tools/host_reference/test_redline_host.py`. They model kernel assertion as CDC
bitmap `0x03`, require the first userspace update to use ioctl mask `0x06` and
produce CDC bitmap `0x00`, require only a redundant RTS clear afterward,
confirm the adapter is limited to open, exercise atomic-ioctl failure cleanup,
and confirm Windows factory selection is unchanged and incompatible Linux
backends fail before open.

The first full unit invocation ran 32 tests with one new-test failure. The
adapter did issue the expected native ioctl mask `0x06`; the fake model
incorrectly applied that mask directly to the distinct CDC bitmap and
therefore calculated `0x03 & ~0x06 = 0x01`. The test was corrected to map Linux
`TIOCM_DTR=0x02` to CDC DTR `0x01` and Linux `TIOCM_RTS=0x04` to CDC RTS
`0x02`. No production code changed in response to that test-model error. The
failure remains recorded here and was not rewritten as a pass.

After correcting the fake model:

| Validation | Actual result | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| `python -m py_compile tools/host_reference/redline_host.py` | Exit 0 | PASS |

### 6.8 Linux Host atomic-open physical verification result

**Capture date/time:** 2026-09-06, `03:01:15.835907`–`03:01:19.625490-05:00`
(`08:01:15.835907`–`08:01:19.625490Z`), approximately 3.790 seconds of

**Linux Host adapter verification: PASS.**

The operator completed the Section 6.7 launcher exactly once and instructed
that neither tty be reopened. This section was produced from the preserved
files only. No tty was opened during analysis, the experiment was not repeated,
and no Host Protocol or Event request was sent. The source under test remained
the Section 6.6 Host utility SHA-256
`3a4d208070f11180424046fe183913dcb8e2b86234b25d1bc711e9fae026a711`.

#### Direct USB and syscall evidence

The Hub-only PCAP contains 50 fully captured records for bus 1 device 7.
tcpdump reports 50 packets captured, 50 received by filter, and zero kernel
drops. The only CDC ACM `SET_CONTROL_LINE_STATE` submissions were:

| PCAP frame | Local time | Setup bytes | CDC value | Completion |
|---:|---|---|---:|---|
| 24 | `03:01:15.966378-05:00` | `21 22 03 00 00 00 00 00` | `0x0003` — DTR=1, RTS=1 | Frame 29 at `15.966439`, status 0 |
| 30 | `03:01:15.967454-05:00` | `21 22 00 00 00 00 00 00` | `0x0000` — DTR=0, RTS=0 | Frame 31 at `15.967566`, status 0 |
| 32 | `03:01:18.997766-05:00` | `21 22 00 00 00 00 00 00` | `0x0000` at close | Frame 33 at `18.998040`, status 0 |

Thus the directly captured opening transition is exactly
`0x0003 -> 0x0000`. The deassertion was submitted 1.076 ms after the assertion.
The later redundant zero is the close operation allowed by the acceptance
criteria. **No `0x0002` request exists anywhere in the capture.**

GDB independently bounds those transfers to the expected calls:

- tty `openat` began at `03:01:15.966264` and returned fd 3 at
  `15.966514`; frame 24's `0x0003` falls within that call;
- the new `TIOCMBIC` with native modem mask bytes `06 00 00 00` began at
  `15.967430`, returned 0 at `15.967623`, and contains frame 30's `0x0000`;
- pyserial's following RTS clear, `TIOCMBIC` mask `04 00 00 00`, returned 0;
  `cdc_acm` emitted no additional request because the CDC state was already 0;
- the ordinary `TCFLSH(TCIFLUSH)` returned 0 and `.open()` returned with
  DTR, RTS, `xonxoff`, `rtscts`, and `dsrdtr` all false;
- tty close began at `18.997674`, emitted frame 32's redundant zero, and
  returned normally at `19.036659`.

The traced tty activity totals one `openat`, eight ioctls, one close, zero
reads and **zero writes**. The PCAP independently contains **zero bulk OUT
transfers**, establishing zero application payload. There is no
`SET_LINE_CODING`, SEND_BREAK, vendor request, or JTAG endpoint traffic.

#### Reset/banner and enumeration evidence

All bulk IN completions before close have zero data length. Pending interrupt
and bulk IN URBs completed with expected `-ENOENT` cancellation only after
close. The passive helper reports:

```text
received_bytes=0
received_hex=""
application_payload_sent=0
```

There was therefore no fresh ESP-ROM output, no
`USB_UART_CHIP_RESET` text, and no boot banner attributable to the open. This
is corroborated by direct absence of any received USB payload, rather than
inferred merely from terminal silence. The helper and GDB process both exited
normally with status 0.

Hub identity was observed before capture, immediately before opening, and
after close with every enumerated field unchanged:

```text
F8:5B:1B:A2:A0:E4
303a:1001
driver cdc_acm
USB path 1-6.3
bus 1 device 7
USEC_INITIALIZED 2728586425
```

path `1-6.4`, bus 1 device 8, initialization ID `2737530481`; it was not

journal observer emitted no message. The udev USB/tty observer emitted only
its startup notice and no device event. The unchanged path, address,
initialization ID and absent udev events directly support stable Hub
enumeration through close; no disconnect, rebind, or re-enumeration was

### 10.10 Physical Event Diagnostics observation and expiry analysis

**Time:** 2026-09-06T04:28:09-05:00. **Disposition:** FAIL / firmware
integration defect identified for terminal-slot reclamation; original delivery
failure remains insufficiently localized. Stage 16 remains FAIL / STOPPED.

Without further input or Host traffic, the operator reported the following
physical Event Diagnostics state exactly as displayed:

```text
Node EVENT DIAG
EV      6/8
SRC/E   10:86032987
ID/FAM  00000001/40
TRY/ST  1/5 EXPIRED
LIFE/PR --/OK

Hub EVENT DIAG
EMPTY
```

No button was pressed, no Host request was sent, no Event was created, and no
firmware or persistent state was changed during this source-only analysis. The
Node display encodes source `0x10`, epoch `0x86032987`, Event ID `0x00000001`,
and BUTTON family `0x40`. The diagnostic firmware is inferred to be running
because this screen exists, but artifact identity and flash evidence were not
reverified in this observation and are not claimed here.

Source establishes the expiry condition. Every durable QUEUED Event receives
the compile-time producer lifetime of 3,600 powered-runtime seconds. While the
Node is powered, the lifetime policy ages every QUEUED slot, not only the
head-of-line Event. For remaining values above 60 seconds it commits a
60-second decrement after each accumulated 60 seconds. Once the remaining
value is 60 seconds or less, accumulated runtime reaching that exact remaining
duration commits QUEUED-to-EXPIRED. Boot recovery independently debits each
QUEUED record by 60 seconds before eligibility; a record already at 60 seconds
or less is committed EXPIRED. Expiry can therefore preempt TX, admission wait,
or backoff and does not require five attempts.

The displayed `1/5` is the durable write-ahead attempt count. It proves exactly
one attempt was armed and committed before any RF start; it is not proof that
one packet completed transmission or that four retries failed. Expiry does not
increment or reset this field. Under continuously healthy, available Event
scheduling with no admission, the 2.5-second admission deadline and bounded
1/2/4/8-second backoffs would normally consume all five attempts long before a
3,600-second expiry. Thus lifetime accounting explains why this record is now
EXPIRED, but by itself does not explain why it remained at one committed
attempt. Reboot debit, extended powered queue residence, or unavailable
delivery/arbitration opportunities could produce this combination; the
current terminal snapshot cannot distinguish them.

`LIFE --` is expected for the current diagnostic presentation. The builder
publishes remaining lifetime only for a QUEUED record or the active delivery
record. FAILED and EXPIRED branches deliberately omit the availability flag,
so the OLED renders `--`; this does not assert that the durable record's stored
remaining-seconds field is zero.

An EXPIRED record is intentionally retained briefly as durable terminal proof:
the reliability design requires a committed QUEUED-to-EXPIRED mutation before
a separate durable EXPIRED-to-FREE reclamation. It therefore legitimately
occupies Node custody between those commits. However, indefinite occupancy is
not intended. `NodeEventStore::ownedCount()` counts every non-FREE record,
including FAILED and EXPIRED. The delivery controller implements
`reclaimTerminal()`, but static inspection finds no production call to it in
`src-rx/main.cpp`; recovered terminal records are also excluded from the
QUEUED index and never become the controller's active head. Consequently
terminal records persist across loops and reboots and permanently consume
slots in the current production integration. This is a Node production
integration defect relative to the specified later-FREE lifecycle, not an
Event codec, identity, or lifetime-policy defect.

The diagnostic record selector first asks for the oldest QUEUED record. Because
it instead displayed an EXPIRED record, the direct source inference is that the
store currently has no QUEUED entries. It then selects the first non-FREE
terminal record by physical slot, so the displayed ID is not necessarily the
last producer identity or a summary of the other five records. `EV 6/8` proves
six non-FREE slots, but this one-page surface cannot reveal whether each of the
other five is FAILED or EXPIRED.

Six accumulated old Events are plausible. NVS Event slots survive ordinary
firmware upload/reset; prior firmware treated every awake non-editor screen
navigation short press as a BUTTON producer; all QUEUED records age in
parallel; and the missing reclamation call preserves every terminal record.
The volatile `PR OK` proves that some producer successfully created an Event
since the current boot, but the OLED does not expose that producer's retained
identity. It therefore does not prove that displayed ID 1 is the Stage 16.5
stimulus or that all six records were created in the latest test.

The Hub EMPTY observation is consistent with no retained ACTIVE or CONSUMED
ledger Event, but it cannot retrospectively prove that the displayed Node Event
never transmitted or that no admission response was attempted. The original
Stage 16.5 producer-noncreation hypothesis is now less likely in general
because Node custody and volatile producer success are directly visible, but
identity correlation to that exact stimulus and the reason only one attempt
committed remain unproven.

**Smallest next action (not executed):** preserve this state and separately
authorize exactly one read-only minor-2 Node `GET_EVENT_DIAGNOSTICS` request.
Its last-producer identity and aggregate enqueue/attempt/expiry/recovery
counters can determine whether the volatile successful producer matches the
displayed Event and sharpen the delivery boundary without mutation. It still
cannot inventory the other five terminal slots. Independently, remediation
requires a narrowly reviewed Node integration change that performs bounded,
safe terminal reclamation only after terminal evidence has been made
observable; merely reflashing or changing settings will not reclaim these NVS
records. No such patch, Host request, flash, or erase is authorized or
performed here. Stage 17 remains NOT EXECUTED.

### 10.11 Read-only Node Event diagnostics and retry-integration analysis

**Time:** 2026-09-06T04:36:45-05:00. **Disposition:** FAIL / Node terminal
reclamation defect remains established; the final armed-attempt completion
boundary remains unresolved. Stage 16 remains FAIL / STOPPED.

The operator completed the previously authorized single read-only minor-2 Node
`GET_EVENT_DIAGNOSTICS` request and reported SUCCESS with these decoded values:

```text
custody_count=6
capacity=8
displayed state=EXPIRED
displayed Event ID=1
family=BUTTON
attempts_used=1
attempts_maximum=5

last_producer.result=CREATED
last_producer.kind=BUTTON
last_producer.Event ID=37

enqueueAccepted=5
eventAttempts=5
eventsExpired=5
successfulAdmissions=0
admissionsAcknowledged=0
attemptsExhausted=0
queueFullRejected=0
persistenceFailures=0
hubCapacityRejections=0
duplicateRetransmissions=0
```

The Hub Event Diagnostics screen continued to show `EMPTY`. No further Host
traffic, button input, physical stimulus, or firmware mutation followed this
read-only request. Raw request/response bytes and per-record identities for the
other five occupied slots were not supplied and are not claimed.

The counter totals do not mean that each of five accepted Events received one
attempt. The delivery controller is strict FIFO and retains one active head
until exact ADMITTED release or terminal handling. No production call reclaims
a FAILED/EXPIRED active head, and `admissionsAcknowledged=0` proves no exact
ADMITTED response released a head during this runtime. Consequently the five
committed `eventAttempts` must belong to one FIFO-head Event; the four later
accepted Events received no attempt while queued behind it. Lifetime policy
ages every QUEUED slot in parallel, explaining how all five accepted Events
could durably expire despite only one of them reaching the delivery engine.
The displayed ID 1/attempt count 1 remains the first retained terminal physical
slot selected by the diagnostic builder and is not the attempted current-boot
head or the last producer, which is explicitly Event ID 37.

Source inspection finds the normal retry calls present. The production loop
invokes `eventDelivery.service(nowMs, synchronousWork)` every iteration while
the subsystem is ready. That call services the 2,500 ms admission deadline and
the 1/2/4/8-second retry-ready deadlines. When READY, the same production
function arbitrates the radio and calls `grantTransmit()`. A successful
RadioLib start calls `txStarted()`; a synchronous start failure calls
`txStartFailed()`. The DIO1 completion flag, while the Event radio owner is
active, calls `txCompleted()` and restores receive. Thus there is no statically
missing call that would generally prevent attempts 2 through 5, and the five
committed attempts physically demonstrate that retry scheduling progressed for
the FIFO head at least four times.

The zero `attemptsExhausted` count narrows the final boundary. After attempt 5,
`txStartFailed()`, a correlated CAPACITY, or a serviced admission timeout would
all call `failedAttempt()` and durably mark the record FAILED, incrementing
`attemptsExhausted`. Exact ADMITTED would increment
`admissionsAcknowledged` and release custody. None occurred. The remaining
source-consistent cases are: the fifth accepted `startTransmit()` never
produced or never had its DIO1 completion recognized, leaving the controller
in TX until lifetime expiry; or the fifth completion/WAIT interval occurred so
near expiry that lifetime expiration preempted the 2,500 ms admission timeout.
A sufficiently long interruption of the production loop between those points
could produce the same ordering. The present diagnostics expose no per-attempt
TX-completion timestamp/state history, so they cannot distinguish these cases.

The production controller has no independent bounded TX-completion deadline.
After RadioLib accepts `startTransmit()`, only the DIO1-owned
`txCompleted()` integration advances TX to WAIT_ADMISSION. If that completion
is missed or misclassified, `service()` continues lifetime accounting but its
TX case has no recovery transition; the Event eventually expires. This is a
real unbounded integration boundary exposed by the evidence, but the current
snapshot does not prove whether the fifth DIO1 event was absent, lost, or
preempted by expiry. Admission timeout and backoff servicing themselves are
present and operational.

Terminal reclamation is a separate confirmed omission in the same broad Node
production-integration layer. Runtime FAILED/EXPIRED heads require the existing
`reclaimTerminal()` call, while terminal records recovered at boot require a
bounded scan and `NodeEventStore::reclaim()` because they are excluded from the
queued index and never become the controller head. Both paths must treat a
reclaim persistence failure as fail-closed degradation. The retry scheduler
does not share this exact cause: attempts 2 through 5 demonstrably progressed,
whereas reclamation is never integrated.

The new evidence explains the Hub `EMPTY`/Host empty-POLL outcome at the
pipeline level: the Node successfully created Events and committed delivery
work, but accepted no ADMITTED response and the Hub retains no ACTIVE or
CONSUMED Event. The failure boundary is therefore after Node producer/custody
and before completed Hub admission. It is no longer plausible that the current
empty Hub is merely waiting for an Event that will retry after expiry. Exact
identity correlation between the original Stage-16.5 button stimulus and the
current-boot Event ID 37 is unavailable, so this does not retroactively prove
which durable record came from that one original press.

**Smallest production remediation (not implemented):** integrate bounded
terminal reclamation in two places: reclaim recovered FAILED/EXPIRED slots
before controller head recovery, and call the existing controller
`reclaimTerminal()` after a runtime terminal commit before loading the next
head. No retry-deadline call is missing and no retry-policy change is justified
by the current evidence. Before changing retry semantics, the smallest
discriminating addition/test is bounded volatile observation of Event TX start,
DIO1 completion classification, `txCompleted()`, WAIT timeout, and retry-ready
transitions for one Event. If a missed completion is reproduced, the narrowest
robustness change would be a specified bounded TX-completion watchdog that
restores receive and consumes the already-committed attempt through the normal
failure/backoff path. That behavior requires separate design authorization;
it is not implemented or assumed here. Stage 17 remains NOT EXECUTED.

### 10.12 Terminal reclamation integration and TX lifecycle instrumentation

**Recorded:** 2026-09-06T05:03:58-05:00. **Disposition:** non-physical
implementation/validation PASS; new candidate physical validation NOT EXECUTED.
Stage 16 remains FAIL / STOPPED. Stage 17 remains NOT EXECUTED.

Branch `main`, tracked base HEAD `088759d835c23f5d70de9247acf82284bf6bab44`,
and the existing uncommitted Event Diagnostics work were preserved. The
operator confirms that the prior diagnostic artifacts were physically flashed:
TX `eb5b20915d0fd80f4a3895ef81573bbb78d020c1ed2817b647415036a8612b67`,
RX `606589278dbf7b434bd3655b4217dfe2c5d22235f5597b7ba40fc9c48d06e63b`.
This is operator-supplied flash evidence, not a new flash or read-back in this
session. The earlier entry describing those artifacts as then unflashed is
preserved chronologically. Boards remain identified by the recorded Hub USB
identity `F8:5B:1B:A2:A0:E4` and Node `F8:5B:1B:A1:0A:E0`; neither tty was
opened and no new hardware identity observation is claimed here.

#### Confirmed fix implemented

Production Node boot now calls a bounded eight-slot terminal sweep after
successful store/identity recovery and before controller FIFO recovery.
Only durably FAILED/EXPIRED records enter the existing store FREE
commit/read-back. Runtime terminal heads enter the existing controller
`reclaimTerminal()` path; non-head records expired in parallel are also swept.
Runtime cleanup acquires standby, rechecks pending RF work, and defers during
TX/preparation, WAIT_ADMISSION, or COMMAND ACK/RESPONSE ownership. Terminal
Event-owned radio work restores receive before cleanup. A reclaim failure
disables the Event subsystem and retains the store's fail-closed degradation;
no failure is treated as free custody.

No QUEUED record is erased. Terminal and FREE remain separate durable commits.
Allocator metadata, Event identity, FIFO ordering, lifetime/debit/checkpoints,
attempt budget, admission correlation, and ACK policy are unchanged. Cleanup
adds the previously missing designed terminal persistence work and allows the
next head to advance; it introduces no new eviction or retention policy.

#### Cross-layer findings and revised hypothesis ranking

1. **Node policy timestamp reversal: source-level defect reproduced natively.**
   `loop()` captures `nowMs` before packet processing. `handleReceivedPacket()`
   later captures a fresh `observedAtMs` and calls `admissionCandidate()` while
   WAIT_ADMISSION, which services lifetime. The same loop subsequently calls
   `serviceNodeEvents(nowMs)` with the older timestamp. Policy's unsigned
   subtraction interprets a small backwards step as nearly 2^32 milliseconds.
   The characterization test services an unrelated candidate at 110 ms then
   the old loop time at 105 ms: both 3600-second queued Events expire, the head
   while WAIT and the other while QUEUED. No RF, real elapsed hour, or missing
   DIO1 is required. Thus the earlier explanation that the physical expirations
   necessarily reflected a real powered-runtime lifetime is not established;
   earlier evidence/interpretations remain unchanged above. The exact physical
   triggering timestamps remain uncaptured. This candidate observes the
   reversal but deliberately preserves the time inputs and policy arithmetic.
2. **Hub idle receive restoration: concrete source-level integration gap.**
   Normal legacy ACK completion calls `radio.standby()` then
   `completeAndScheduleNextTransaction()`/`scheduleNextTransaction()`, which
   only sets logical IDLE and the next deadline. `finishHostStructuredOwnership()`
   similarly calls standby and sets IDLE. The idle transport branch returns
   until the next command deadline without calling receive. The Hub therefore
   can be logically IDLE but physically in standby between ordinary exchanges.
   Setup, Event ACK completion, and a local Host operation do restore receive;
   this is not a claim that the Hub never receives. The gap directly explains
   how healthy legacy transactions can coexist with missed unsolicited Events
   and an empty ledger. Hub radio behavior is not changed in this candidate.
3. **Node ownership overwrite/preemption: source-supported conditional path.**
   Event arbitration selects EVENT_TX, but the general runtime phase remains
   LISTENING. Node Host service and settings maintenance gate on that phase
   without checking Event ownership; standby/startListening can interrupt TX
   and replace the arbiter owner before the shared DIO1 flag is classified.
   A native arbiter/observer test records the resulting dispatch discrepancy
   without changing ownership policy. Earlier evidence reports no additional
   Host requests during the failure, so this alone does not establish the
   historical cause. No Host/maintenance policy change is implemented here.
4. **Missing/coalesced DIO1 completion: unresolved, weaker than the paths above.**
   Pinned local RadioLib 7.7.1 `PhysicalLayer::startTransmit()` stages TX then
   launches it. SX126x staging maps TX_DONE|TIMEOUT status but only TX_DONE to
   DIO1, writes the complete payload into the chip FIFO, then clears IRQ flags.
   Launch uses SET_TX with no chip TX timeout and waits for BUSY/PA ramp-up;
   accepted start is not RF completion. `setDio1Action()` attaches a rising-edge
   ISR on the existing pin. Events and legacy ACK/RESPONSE use this same path.
   The local Event frame buffer is copied synchronously before the call returns,
   so its stack lifetime is not an asynchronous-buffer defect. Production uses
   one shared boolean completion flag and dispatches by current owner, not raw
   SX1262 IRQ status. Receive staging enters standby, clears IRQ flags, and
   remaps RX IRQs. Flags can coalesce or be cleared by existing paths. There is
   no Event-specific IRQ mask discrepancy established by source inspection and
   no independent TX completion watchdog. No watchdog or IRQ policy was added.

Taken together, Hub receive gaps plus Node false expiry provide a concrete
source-supported explanation of zero admissions and five expirations after
five committed head attempts. This is not retrospective physical proof of
each prior transition. A fresh identity with lifecycle evidence is still
needed. The fact that the prior displayed ID 1 is distinct from last producer
ID 37 remains unchanged; original Stage-16 stimulus identity correlation is
still unavailable. This investigation used local production/dependency source
and native characterization, not a new radio trace or external research.

Counter ownership also limits the earlier inference: successfulAdmissions,
hubCapacityRejections, and duplicateRetransmissions are incremented by Hub
components, not by the Node delivery controller. Their zero values in a Node
response do not independently prove no Hub admission, no CAPACITY reply, or no
retransmission. Node admissionsAcknowledged is the exact-ADMITTED-release
counter; the physical Hub EMPTY observation supplies separate ledger evidence.
The new decoded/matched ACK counters and retained status make Node response
observation explicit. A final correlated CAPACITY before expiry would still
have driven attempt 5 to FAILED, so the zero exhaustion observation remains
relevant without treating the role-inapplicable capacity counter as evidence.

#### New bounded observations

The Node has a volatile observer with thirty saturating uint16 counters:
READY selection, arbitration grant, start called/accepted/failed, controller
TX entry, DIO1 inside/outside controller-TX window, Event completion dispatch,
other-path dispatch during TX, txCompleted invocation, WAIT entry, admission
response decoded/matched, timeout, BACKOFF entry/deadline, READY retry, expiry
from QUEUED/READY/TX/WAIT/BACKOFF/TX_PREPARE, reversed policy time, receive/Host/
maintenance during TX, pending flag cleared during TX, and terminal reclaimed.
It retains last encoded TX identity/attempt, current controller and owner,
last start result, foreground observation/start/completion times, decoded
admission status, and both inputs of the last reversed policy-time observation.
No retained observation is read by scheduling or persistence decisions.

ISR instrumentation only increments two saturating uint32 totals and sets the
existing flag. There is no extra SPI read, radio call, logging, interrupt mask,
sleep, or timer. Totals are narrowed to uint16 in snapshots; independently
sampled ISR fields are not an atomic history of every edge. DIO1 attribution
uses a volatile diagnostic controller-TX window independent of mutable radio
ownership. Raw chip IRQ status is not sampled. Physical timing overhead of
instrumentation has not yet been characterized.

The existing NONE/111-byte schema-1 operation is unchanged. Minor-2 local
`GET_EVENT_DIAGNOSTICS` additionally accepts UNSIGNED_32 selector 1 and returns

Counters are volatile current-runtime aggregates, not per-Event traces.
The retained last encoded TX identity is
`0x10 : 0x85032967 : 0x00000047` (Event ID 71 decimal), attempt 4.
This agrees with OLED LAST/T, but does not assign all twelve attempts, both
clock reversals, or all expirations to that identity. No correlation with the
original Stage-16.5 stimulus or its failed POLL is established by this run.

#### Interpretation of the timestamp and lifecycle evidence

1. **Timestamp reversal is now physically observed, not merely hypothetical.**
   The observer samples the time supplied to the lifetime policy. It recorded
   two reversed-input detections, retaining the last pair 224678 -> 224677 ms.
   This is a one-millisecond backwards policy input, not a normal uint32 wrap
   and not evidence that the hardware clock itself ran backwards. In the
   inspected `Policy::service()`, unsigned subtraction from the prior tracked
   baseline yields 4,294,967,295 ms (about 49.7 days); the uint64 accumulator
   preserves that value. Successful checkpoint/expiry processing therefore
   exhausts the remaining lifetime of records tracked across that pair.
   Production packet handling supplies fresh observedAtMs to
   `admissionCandidate()`, then the same loop supplies its older nowMs to
   `serviceNodeEvents()`. The existing native characterization reproduces
   exactly this failure class, including expiry of the waiting head and queued
   followers. Physical reversed inputs plus two WAIT and six QUEUED expirations
   provide high-confidence evidence that this defect is operating in production.
   The snapshot is not a per-call/per-slot log: only the last reversal pair is
   retained, so exact allocation of all eight expirations between the two
   reversals, prior recovery, or other times is not directly observed. It is
   no longer justified to explain these expirations solely by an actual hour
   of powered runtime or reboot debit.
2. **TX start/completion integration and retries progressed in this runtime.**
   Twelve start calls were accepted, twelve controller TX entries occurred,
   twelve DIO1 observations occurred in the Event-TX diagnostic window, twelve
   were classified by the Event-owner foreground branch, twelve txCompleted()
   calls occurred, and twelve transitions entered WAIT_ADMISSION. There is no
   aggregate completion deficit or reproduced stuck-TX boundary here. DIO1
   counts/classification are software observations, not sampled SX1262 IRQ
   registers or proof of RF reception at the Hub. Ten admission timeouts and
   nine BACKOFF entry/deadline/retry-READY transitions prove the production
   timeout and retry scheduler is being serviced; missing retry integration is
   not supported. The twelve WAIT entries are consistent with ten timeouts
   plus two WAIT expirations. The extra timeout without BACKOFF is consistent
   with terminal attempt-budget failure under `failedAttempt()`, not evidence
   that a retry was lost; the supplied lifecycle page does not independently
   enumerate that terminal FAILED record.
3. **Terminal reclamation is physically exercised.** Node custody is now 0/8,
   and terminal_reclaimed=15 counts successful terminal-to-FREE operations,
   incremented only after the existing store commit/read-back returns success.
   Together with the prior six-slot backlog and operator-reported flash, this
   confirms restored capacity and successful boot/runtime reclamation, rather
   than continuing permanent occupation by terminal records. Fifteen is a
   cumulative operation count across reused slots, not fifteen simultaneous
   slots or fifteen fresh BUTTON Events. Six recovered terminals plus eight
   new expirations plus one attempt-budget failure is a consistent accounting,
   not an identity-resolved history. No reboot-after-reclaim or power-failure
   durability test was performed. EXPIRED in the lifecycle page with OLED
   EMPTY is expected: reclaim clears active custody, while the controller's
   terminal state and volatile last-TX evidence can remain until another head
   is loaded. It does not mean Event 71 still occupies a slot.
4. **No observed TX contamination from the diagnostic query.** OLED already
   showed 12/12/12 before Host access, and the post-activity read reports
   host_during_tx=0. Maintenance/receive during TX, pending-flag clearing during
   TX, and other-path DIO dispatch during TX are also zero. These observations
   exclude the instrumented interference paths during these observed attempts;
   they do not assert that Host access has no radio side effects outside TX.
5. **Hub admission remains unproven and was absent from the observed ledger.**
   Hub shows zero ACTIVE and zero CONSUMED at observation. Node decoded no
   valid admission response during WAIT and matched none to an attempt. That
   does not prove no Hub RF packet arrived, no rejection was transmitted, or
   that a particular Hub radio mode caused every loss. Unrelated successfully
   read packets can enter admissionCandidate() and advance lifetime before
   admission decoding fails, so zero decoded admission ACKs is fully compatible
   with the fresh-RX/old-loop timestamp path. Hub's actual radio mode, Event RX
   dispatch, validation/admission decisions, and ACK transmission during these
   attempts were not captured.

#### Revised ranking and next authorized-work boundary

- **Established production defect, highest confidence for premature expiry:**
  Node reversed policy timestamps, now physically reproduced. Fixing lifetime
  input ordering is justified without another failing Event stimulus.
- **Leading remaining explanation for absent Hub admission:** the concrete
  Hub receive-restoration gap at legacy/structured transaction release. Source
  leaves the chip in standby while logically IDLE; setup, Event ACK completion,
  and local Host service can restore receive. Hub EMPTY and zero Node admission
  ACKs are consistent with this defect but do not physically establish the
  mode during any particular transmission. Confidence is high in the source
  defect, not in an exact per-attempt causal attribution. Other RF reception,
  receive dispatch/validation, or admission/ACK-path failures remain unresolved
  alternatives, without positive evidence here.
- **Not reproduced, substantially downgraded:** missing/misclassified DIO1 or
  txCompleted(), and Host/maintenance ownership interference. The complete
  12/12 progression and zero interference observations argue against these
  explanations for this runtime. Aggregate counters do not rule out every
  intermittent race in other runs, but provide no basis for adding a watchdog.
- **Confirmed prior defect, observed repair working:** terminal reclamation.
  It is not the remaining cause of an empty Hub in this snapshot. Retry
  scheduling also demonstrably progresses; no retry-policy change is justified.

The originally planned clean one-Event test is **not required before seeking
authorization for the two narrow source-supported repairs**. Repeating the
unfixed run would not add necessary proof of timestamp reversal and, without

A controlled identity-correlated single-Event test remains required **after
repair** to prove the vertical slice; the multi-Event counters cannot replace
that test or satisfy Stage 16. Prior failed identities cannot be reconstructed
by creating another Event.

The operator authorized exactly the two integration repairs supported by
Section 10.13. Local preflight confirmed branch `main`, tracked base HEAD
`088759d835c23f5d70de9247acf82284bf6bab44`, and the expected uncommitted Event
Diagnostics/reclamation work. All that work was preserved. The last reported
flashed candidate remains the Section 10.12 pair, physically characterized in
10.13; the outputs below supersede its local build files, not the historical
flash evidence. Neither tty was opened or rediscovered during implementation.
No Host traffic, Event stimulus, reset, upload, commit, or remote Git action
was performed. No firmware metadata, dependency, board, or system configuration
was changed. Tool versions observed: PlatformIO Core 6.1.19, Python 3.14.6.

#### Node repair: remove the stale production time input

`serviceNodeEvents()` no longer accepts the loop-start timestamp. A small
shared/testable `NodeEventDelivery::serviceNow()` boundary samples `millis()`
when Event service actually runs, after packet processing and its fresh
admission-candidate time. It services the existing controller with that sample
and returns the sample for consistent use by the remaining work in the same
Event-service invocation. No policy call inside that invocation interleaves a
later policy timestamp before reusing the sampled value.

The original lifetime policy arithmetic and controller transitions are
unchanged. There is no clamp, saved-time maximum, reversal suppression, counter
reset, or fabricated elapsed time. CLOCK_REVERSED remains intact and a negative
control still deliberately feeds the old bad ordering directly to the policy
to prove that the diagnostic and false-expiry characterization were not hidden.
Only the production source of that bad ordering is corrected.

Four new native tests cover the physical 224677 loop / 224678 packet pair,
a packet-processing interval 105 -> 110 -> 112, and legitimate uint32 wrap
FFFFFFFE -> 00000002 -> 00000003. They exercise the same serviceNow helper
used by production, with a WAIT_ADMISSION head and queued follower. They verify
zero reversals/expirations, exact unchanged persistent record bytes/identity
writes, FIFO head and attempt counts, and unchanged admission timeout/backoff
boundaries. Monotonic service also retains the sixty-second reboot debit,
checkpoint boundary, and genuine powered-lifetime expiry for both records.

#### Hub repair: one-shot RX restoration at IDLE boundaries

The shared release helper is now called after the existing phase/deadline
updates at:

- legacy terminal completion, including success, remote error, invalid ACK
  status, encode failure, and exhausted attempts through the existing common
  completion function;
- the legacy retry-delay IDLE exit, retaining its transaction/retry state and
  existing 500 ms schedule; and
- structured ownership release, reached through terminal response/ACK handling
  and the existing remote lifecycle timeout/abort release path.

The 3000 ms next-transaction interval, 500 ms retry interval, sequence/retry
updates, ACK wait policy, and structured deadlines were not changed. Release
restoration does not reschedule them or restart receive on each idle loop.
The former unconditional standby calls at these exits were moved into the
guarded restoration boundary, so they cannot preempt an already pending IRQ
before the new checks run.

The helper refuses to touch the radio while either Event-ACK ownership
indicator is active, preserves an already pending IRQ, acquires standby, and
rechecks pending IRQ after successful standby. It reuses the existing
`restoreHubReceive` with an explicit no-clear option; all pre-existing callers
retain its original default behavior. No flag is cleared by the release path,
including an IRQ raised during RX start. Pending work remains for existing
dispatch. Arbiter owner/deadline metadata is not rewritten. The Event admission
ACK completion body and its original receive restoration remain unchanged.

Standby failure and receive-start failure are distinct helper results. Both
increment radioErrors, set DEGRADED health, record RADIO_START_RECEIVE, and
show RX START FAILED with the failing release step. If standby fails while an
IRQ also arrives, the error is still reported and the flag remains set. There
is no recursive/automatic restoration retry; the normal scheduled transaction
path remains available. A failure is not represented as successful RX.

Ten new native release-boundary tests cover legacy/structured owner contexts,
pending IRQ before/during standby, a consumed triggering completion, a new IRQ
during receive start, both Event ownership guards and saved metadata, standby
and RX failure, and simultaneous standby failure/IRQ arrival. Eight additional
Python source-wiring checks verify that the production call sites use these
tested helpers, retain scheduling/failure exits, preserve the Event-ACK path,
and introduce no continuous idle-loop restart. These are source-level checks
plus injected radio/clock tests, not execution of Arduino main loops against
real SX1262 hardware or a complete hardware race proof.

#### Supplied physical exercise and observations

The operator cycled through the ordinary UI. Short presses from RADIO onward
produce BUTTON Events under the existing semantics, resulting in **five
Events**, not the planned single stimulus. This is a useful multi-Event
repair-validation run, **not a pristine one-Event Stage-16 execution** and not
execution of the Stage-17 sequential-FIFO/fault matrix.

The Hub fields above preserve the supplied values, including its described
source/epoch/identity, rather than claiming a raw screenshot. Source confirms
ACT/CON is ACTIVE / CONSUMED / capacity, ORD/ST is admission ordinal / state,
and ADM/D/M is successfulAdmissions / duplicates / identity-content mismatches.
The Hub primary record is the oldest ACTIVE admission. Node EV reads owned
custody; RELEASED can remain after the slot becomes FREE. LIFE `--` means no
remaining lifetime is exposed for a retained custody record, not expiry in
this RELEASED/empty state. Producer `OK` is a retained volatile CREATED result,
not five separately captured producer outcomes.
