#### Interpretation and evidence limits

1. **Node policy-time repair: physical repair-validation PASS in this run.**
   Zero observed clock reversals, all six expiry counters zero, and five
   completed admission cycles show the prior reversed-time/false-expiry failure
   was not reproduced on the repaired production path. The physical results
   complement the source-order repair and regression tests in 10.14; this is
   not merely hiding the counter. The zero retained reversal pair means no
   reversed pair was captured, not that policy time stopped at zero. No trace
   proves the exact old one-millisecond ordering opportunity occurred in this
   run, and no hour-long lifetime, wrap, or reboot-debit test was performed.
2. **Hub receive-restoration repair: functional physical validation PASS.**
   Five ACTIVE records and successfulAdmissions=5 expose successful durable
   new admissions. In `HubEventLedger::Ledger::admit()`, that counter and ACTIVE
   state follow successful commit/read-back; `HubAdapter` prepares ADMITTED only
   after the durable result (or exact retained duplicate). Node decoded and
   exactly matched five responses, without timeout or retry. Thus the Hub RF
   receive -> dispatch -> validation -> admission -> admission-ACK path
   operated successfully in this runtime, before any reported Hub POLL.
   This is source-backed durable-admission evidence, not an independent NVS
   read or reboot/power-loss durability test. There is still no direct Hub
   radio-mode/release-boundary trace identifying which legacy or structured
   exit preceded each Event. The functional repair passes without claiming
   physical branch coverage of every restored exit or isolation of the two
   repairs' contributions to a specific historical failure.
3. **Complete production-to-release progression is supported for the batch.**
   Operator BUTTON activity, five READY selections/grants/accepted starts/TX
   entries, five Event-window DIO1 observations/classifications, five
   txCompleted calls/WAIT entries, five Hub new admissions, five exact ACK
   matches, and Node custody 0/8 form a consistent successful lifecycle.
   `matchesEvent()` checks reversed endpoints, RF sequence, family, epoch,
   and ID, not just Host request ID. Status 0 is exact ADMITTED. Node RELEASED
   is entered only after matching ADMITTED and successful durable
   releaseQueued; failures/rejections are not labeled RELEASED. ACK_MATCHED
   alone is not a durable-release counter and can also count rejection
   statuses; the additional Hub, RELEASED, empty-custody, zero terminal-reclaim,
   zero expiry, and zero retry observations are essential to this conclusion.
   Direct retained ADMITTED/RELEASED evidence pertains to last TX ID 0x65;
   five successful first-attempt releases are the source-backed aggregate
   interpretation, not five separately logged ACK statuses/store commits.
4. **Identity and timing scope remain bounded.** Hub displays
   `0x10:0x85032967:0x00000061` (oldest, ordinal 1, BUTTON `0x40`); Node
   retains `0x10:0x85032967:0x00000065` (last TX, attempt 1). Five sequential
   IDs 0x61..0x65 are consistent with the observations and FIFO/allocator
   semantics; intermediate IDs/bodies/ordinals were not individually read.
   Different displayed endpoints are expected, not an identity mismatch.
   All lifecycle counters span five Events; they must not all be assigned to
   ID 0x65. Its retained start-to-completion-call interval is 233 ms. The last
   observation is 214 ms after completion (447 ms after start); without
   last_step this is not labeled a directly captured ACK-arrival timestamp
   or RF airtime. No full per-Event timestamp or packet trace exists.
5. **No instrumented TX interference was observed.** OLED already showed
   5/5/5 before the post-activity Host query; host_during_tx and every supplied
   receive/maintenance/flag-clearing/other-path interference counter are zero.
   The query therefore did not contaminate these observed TX attempts through
   those paths. It does not qualify arbitrary Host/maintenance concurrency.
6. **Terminal reclamation remains a previously validated repair, not a newly
   exercised transition here.** terminal_reclaimed=0 is expected when Events
   finish by ADMITTED -> FREE, with no FAILED/EXPIRED records to reclaim.
   Custody 0/8 shows no present backlog. Keep the 10.13 physical reclaim=15
   evidence and 10.14 native regression results; do not manufacture another
   expiry/reboot simply to increment this counter.

#### Revised root-cause disposition

| Hypothesis / defect | Disposition after this run |
|---|---|
| Node reversed policy-time ordering | Confirmed and previously physically reproduced defect; source repaired; no recurrence/false expiry in five successful post-repair cycles. Bounded repair validation PASS. |
| Hub IDLE/standby receive-restoration gap | Source-established defect repaired; successful physical receive/admission/ACK behavior supports functional repair PASS. High confidence in the source defect; no retroactive per-attempt Hub standby proof or isolated causal experiment. |
| Missing/misclassified DIO1 or txCompleted | Not reproduced; earlier 12/12 and now 5/5 completion/WAIT progress substantially downgrade it. DIO counters are software observations, not chip IRQ-register captures. No watchdog justified. |
| Missing retry servicing / broken retry policy | Earlier timeouts/backoffs/retries demonstrated servicing. This run needed none; it neither newly exercises retry nor supports changing its policy. |
| Node Host/maintenance ownership interference | Not observed in either characterized run; known source risk remains outside the two repairs. Zero counters do not qualify deliberate concurrent access. |
| Missing terminal reclamation | Confirmed defect already repaired and physically observed working; not a remaining blocker here. Current success uses admission release, not terminal reclamation. |

Neither this result nor the earlier characterization reconstructs the exact
failure path of original HELLO `0x1601` or empty POLL `0x1660`. Prior FAIL
records, the original possible early-POLL interpretation, and the progression
of earlier hypotheses remain unchanged. There is no current unexplained
admission failure requiring another firmware change.

### 11.2 Persistent-state baseline — recommendation pending confirmation

The most recent Stage-16 evidence implies, **not freshly measures**, this state:

- Node: custody 0/8, last controller observation RELEASED, last TX
  `0x10:0x85032967:0x00000065`, attempt 1. Last-TX/producer observations and
  counters are volatile and may differ after an unreported reboot/activity.
- Hub: four ACTIVE records, IDs `0x62..0x65`, and one CONSUMED proof, ID
  `0x61`, all under source `0x10` / epoch `0x85032967`. The count 4/1 is
  inferred from five admissions and one successful consume; oldest ACTIVE
  0x62 was directly returned by 0x1807. Do not treat this inference as a new
  diagnostic snapshot or assume unseen intervening activity did not occur.

The smallest non-erasing baseline preparation would be to establish current
OLED/idle state before Node Host access, then use the validated safe Host
transport for read-only diagnostics on both roles. With Node idle/empty,
archive each Hub oldest ACTIVE record in full using POLL and explicitly
CONSUME its exact returned nine-byte identity, one at a time with fresh request
IDs. If the state matches the history, this drains only IDs 0x62..0x65; do not
blindly consume an assumed range or any unexpected record. Preserve each raw/
decoded response and the chronological transition. Stop on any unexplained
identity/count/result discrepancy. An unexpected Node backlog must be observed
and localized, not cleared or allowed to expire as a shortcut to a passing
baseline. No such preparation request or mutation has been executed here.

After those four supported consumes, expected state is **Node custody=0;
Hub ACTIVE=0, CONSUMED=5, EMPTY slots=3**, with a successful empty POLL.
This is an empty outstanding-custody/Host-delivery baseline, **not a literally
empty Hub ledger**. It preserves canonical Event proofs and source/ordinal
allocation state; the existing Stage-16 evidence remains preserved even though
later explicitly recorded consumption would change live custody.

Source boundary: `HubEventLedger::Ledger::consume()` commits ACTIVE->CONSUMED,
never EMPTY. `admit()` uses `firstEmpty()` and, only if needed,
`oldestReclaimable()` for an earlier same-source CONSUMED proof; there is no
public drain-to-EMPTY operation or automatic tombstone ageing. Empty slots
are preferred, and even admission of a newer Event does not empty all older
proofs. `testEmptyPreferredBeforeTombstoneReplacement` and
`testOldestLegalSameSourceTombstoneReplacement` explicitly exercise this.
Reboot recovers proofs. Settings reset affects the separate `redline`
namespace, not Event storage `red_evt`; it is not a valid Event-store clear.
No full-flash/NVS erase, identity reset, settings reset, or storage replacement
is proposed as routine qualification preparation.

Section 23's Hub-present setup says **"empty stores"**. Therefore use of the
non-erasing 0-custody/0-ACTIVE baseline with five explicitly retained CONSUMED
proofs requires an explicit operator/qualification-authority clarification
before executing that scenario. It must not be silently recorded as eight
EMPTY Hub slots. This is the smallest recommended baseline, with three
literal EMPTY slots and safe later-proof replacement available. If literal
EMPTY on every Hub slot is required, no existing production operation can
establish it from these records; stop for a separately scoped, evidence-
preserving storage-initialization plan rather than inventing a clear command
or erasing storage. No normative text was changed to resolve this ambiguity.

### 11.3 Scenario execution surfaces and limitations

Classification below is a **source-based feasibility assessment**, not an
executed physical result. All scenario results remain NOT EXECUTED. Production
`tx`/`rx` have no selective admission-ACK drop switch, pre-commit pause, forced
Event retransmission, or ledger-clear operation. Event diagnostics are local
read-only observations, not fault controls. The storage/Host compile fixtures
and trace/capability build environments are not physical Event-loss fixtures;
they do not justify replacing the qualified production binaries.

| Section-23 scenario | Existing execution surface / boundary |
|---|---|
| 1. Hub present | Production BUTTON or MANUAL_CHECK_IN producer, OLED and local Host diagnostics/POLL. Physically executable after the empty-store baseline clarification above. Record one controlled new identity; do not carry the five-Event Stage-16 batch forward as this scenario. |
| 2. Hub absent/restored | Operator removes/restores all Hub power; Node timeout/backoff and admission observations. Physically executable, but restore before the five-attempt budget is exhausted; ordinary chat latency is not a timing control. |
| 3. Lost admission ACK | No existing production selective RF-loss control or physical qualification fixture found. Native Hub adapter/ledger tests provide deterministic logic evidence. Exact physical loss needs independently evidenced selective loss; an absent Hub does not prove admission-before-ACK-loss. Record a physical limitation unless actually induced. |
| 4. Node reboot pending | Production power cycle can preserve a QUEUED follower behind an unresolved FIFO head before that follower's first TX; record which identity and its pre-reboot attempt state. Cutting power between a first enqueue and immediate first TX is not a deterministic manual control. If the pending/pre-TX boundary cannot be evidenced, record that limitation; native queue/recovery tests cover the exact state. |
| 5. Node reboot during retry | Operator power cycle during observed WAIT/BACKOFF while Hub unavailable; existing diagnostics establish identity/attempts before and after. Physically executable with a bounded timed action; precise sub-state at power removal must be evidenced, not assumed. |
| 6. Hub reboot after admission | Exact admission-commit-before-Node-success setup shares the selective-ACK-loss boundary in scenario 3. No production pause/drop fixture; native recovery/duplicate tests available. Ordinary reboot of an already RELEASED Event is scenario 7, not proof of this case. |
| 7. Hub reboot ACTIVE | Retain known ACTIVE identity/ordinal, manually reboot Hub, re-read diagnostics/POLL. Physically executable with existing production surfaces. |
| 8. Hub reboot CONSUMED | Consume a recorded identity, manually reboot Hub, retry exact consume with a fresh Host request ID. This production path is physically executable. Exact RF retransmission of an already RELEASED Node Event has no supported producer/replay control; the retransmission subcase has existing native proof, not a supplied physical RF fixture. |
| 9. Host disconnect after POLL | Reference utility closes after a successful POLL; reconnect/POLL without consumption. Existing validated transport suffices; do not disconnect board power to simulate a Host-only disconnect. |
| 10. Lost consume response | Existing CLI waits for a response and has no one-response-drop option. A separately reviewed bounded Host-only receive/discard wrapper using `open_serial_transport()` could model loss without firmware changes; none was implemented/executed here. Native service/ledger tests already cover durable idempotence independent of delivery. A normal successful consume/retry is not physical response-loss evidence; short timeout or timed cable pull alone does not prove the commit boundary. |
| 11. Node saturation | Hub unavailable; controlled eligible producer gestures to retain eight slots and reject ninth before FIFO-head exhaustion/reclaim frees capacity. Production OLED/diagnostics expose counts and rejection. Physically executable with bounded timing; arbitrary slow screen cycling is not a controlled saturation test. |
| 12. Hub saturation | Retain eight ACTIVE identities, then one further Node Event; observe exact CAPACITY and intact existing records. Production supports this. Any starting CONSUMED proofs must be counted/replaced according to normal policy; eight total occupied slots is not proof of eight ACTIVE slots. |
| 13. Sequential FIFO | Controlled multiple producer gestures, Node FIFO/Hub ordinal observations and oldest-first POLL/explicit consume. Physically executable. Reboot-reserved ID/ordinal gaps are allowed; do not assume every new identity is previous+1 after a reboot. |
| 14. Interleaved COMMAND | Existing remote structured operations through Hub while Node Event pending. Physically executable; record Event/COMMAND identities and original deadlines. Node-local Host reads can interfere with TX: prefer OLED and post-activity lifecycle snapshots, and treat any unexplained ownership/deadline deviation as STOP. |
| 15. UI/input/storage coexistence | Existing navigation/settings and ordinary safe-point storage work during Event load. Physically executable; explicitly count intended producer gestures and settings actions. No hidden gesture or settings reset is needed. |
| 16. Legacy structured regression | Existing Host minor-1/minor-2 v0.6 operation set over Hub->Node RF. Physically executable with operation-specific physical observations where required. |
| 17. Legacy TEST/ACK | Autonomous established TEST traffic with Host absent; existing counters/presentation and source/native Wire regression evidence. Physically executable. Counters alone must not be called a captured on-air byte trace. |

Existing deterministic fixtures are **native**, not on-device fault switches:

- `test/test_event_radio_integration`: commit-before-ACK and
  `testAckStartLossKeepsCustodyAndRetryRegeneratesAck` exercise regenerated
  acknowledgment and unchanged ledger custody.
- `test/test_hub_event_ledger`: `testLostAckAndBothRebootRetainsSingleActiveProof`
  synthetically retains/replays the same Event across Hub recovery; it is not
  a physical Node+Hub reset/RF trace. CONSUMED duplicate/recovery, write/read-
  back failure, and torn-replacement cases exercise bounded ledger behavior.
- `test/test_host_event_service`: durable consume/idempotence, repeated POLL,
  and ACTIVE/CONSUMED recovery tests call the service over fake storage. They
  do not prove actual USB response loss.
- `test/test_node_event_store`, `test/test_node_event_delivery`, and record/
  identity tests cover commit/read-back faults, recovery/FIFO, write-ahead
  attempts, checkpoint/debit, terminal reclaim, and boundary timing.

These were included in the recorded 812/812 native PASS in 10.14 and were
inspected, not rerun here. Precise Node/Hub torn-write interruption is not
deterministically controllable with a manual cable pull or supported production
Host request. Section 23 explicitly permits native fault injection to remain
authoritative for that exact logic boundary, with the physical limitation
recorded. Practical evidenced pre/post-commit power-cycle tests remain physical
work; never claim an actual torn write merely because a board was unplugged.

Selective loss and the required lost-ACK/both-reboot sequence remain separate
physical coverage limitations, not automatically waived by the torn-write
clause. Section 8's limitation policy and Section 26's release blockers must
be reconciled explicitly at qualification closeout; listing native coverage
does not turn an unexecuted physical scenario into PASS or authorize release.
No new firmware injection mechanism, watchdog, retry policy, RF change, or
dependency is proposed. Do not remove an antenna or generate RF interference
as a substitute for controlled loss.

The screen sequence was expected; the EMPTY state is not the expected exact-ACK

additional eligible Event, Hub request, reset, flash, or firmware change was
performed during this OLED-only investigation.

| Node OLED field | Baseline in 11.7 | New observation | Meaning / limit |
|---|---|---|---|
| EV | 0/8 | 0/8 | No currently owned custody records reported |
| STATE | RELEASED | EMPTY | No record is displayed; exact-ACK release is not established |
| LIFE/PR | --/OK | --/OK | No displayed lifetime; volatile last-producer status maps CREATED to OK |
| TX/D/C | 5/5/5 | 6/6/6 | Accepted starts / Event-window DIO1 observations / Event-completion classifications each increased by one |
| LAST/T | 00000065/1 | 00000066/1 | Retained last-TX Event ID 0x66, committed attempt 1; source/epoch are not shown by these rows |

Precision: the OLED `C` is **DIO_CLASSIFIED_EVENT**, not the separate
TX_COMPLETED_CALLED or WAIT_ENTERED counter. Earlier conversational shorthand
calling this a completion count must be read as completion classification;
neither callback count nor WAIT entry was newly measured here. Full counters
remain aggregate, but the +1/+1/+1 deltas and new retained ID are consistent
with the single controlled stimulus, with no additional producer gestures
requested or reported. No new full nine-byte identity is yet captured.

Source interpretation (`buildEventDetailSnapshot()` in `src-rx/main.cpp`,
EVENT_DIAGNOSTICS rendering in `lib/device_ui/src/device_ui.h`, and
`NodeEventDelivery::Controller`): when no non-FREE record is found, the detail
state remains its default EMPTY unless the controller state is RELEASED.
Exact ADMITTED commits release and sets RELEASED; terminal reclaim commits
FREE and removes the active record while retaining FAILED/EXPIRED controller
state, which the no-record OLED path therefore presents as EMPTY. Thus EMPTY
is compatible with terminal reclamation and cannot distinguish FAILED from
EXPIRED. `LIFE --` here means no displayed record/lifetime, not independent
proof of expiry. This is source interpretation of observed presentation,
not a new direct measurement of terminal state or NVS writes.

The added accepted start, DIO1 observation/classification, and last ID 0x66
strongly narrow the failure beyond a producer that never created an Event,
no TX start, or a wholly missing/misclassified DIO1 for this observed attempt.
The production path builds that attempt from queued custody after a committed
attempt increment; this is code-supported custody history, not a captured
transient QUEUED screen. Current retained Node custody/pending retry is not
supported by EV 0/8. Terminal failure/expiry followed by reclaim is the leading
source-consistent explanation for EMPTY, but the exact outcome is unresolved.
Do not assert attempt exhaustion, an admission rejection, timestamp reversal,
Host interference, or successful ACK release without the missing diagnostics.

Hub observation 0x1910 remains ACTIVE=0 / CONSUMED=5 / derived EMPTY=3,
successfulAdmissions=5 at its earlier timestamp. It has not been re-read, so
later Hub state is unknown. Together these observations do not satisfy the
Hub-present gate and do not establish permanent Event loss or its exact cause.

### 12.9 Authorized stale-slot lifetime remediation — native/fixture validation only

**Recorded 2026-09-06, 13:54-05:00 (Dreadnought).** Following the source-only
review and deterministic regression preparation, the operator explicitly
authorized the minimal production stale-slot lifetime repair and non-physical
validation. No hardware access, production TX/RX rebuild, flash, reset,
physical Event, Scenario 2, or change to the unresolved OLED/Host discrepancy
was authorized or performed. Branch remains `main`, tracked base HEAD
`19ecbc3d864aa82295990433725fc63b50fadef6`. At entry, this record already
contained uncommitted chronological additions and the delivery test file
contained the five new regression cases; those changes were preserved.

**Confirmed defect, separate from Event 0x66 attribution:** volatile policy
timing is indexed by physical custody slot. Exact ADMITTED release, or FAILED
terminal reclamation, can free a slot while its policy runtime remains tracked.
The empty-queue early return in `Controller::service()` does not run policy
cleanup. Before this repair, the common production creation sink durably
enqueued a fresh Event without invoking the existing `Policy::trackEnqueued()`.
A new occupant could therefore inherit the old baseline and accumulated
milliseconds, including monotonic idle time greater than its entire lifetime.
No clock reversal is needed for that failure. Cleaning only the empty-queue
service path would also miss FREE-to-QUEUED reuse between policy ticks and
would not establish lifetime at the actual new enqueue boundary.

The preceding regression-only run had 42 Unity cases: 38 passed and the four
integrated slot-reuse cases failed. Long idle (3,600,001 ms) produced EXPIRED /
effective remaining 0; ordinary idle (2,000 ms) wrongly reduced the new Event
to 3,598 seconds. Attempts and clock-reversal count were zero. The fifth,
explicit per-slot policy-tracking control passed. The native program exited
with the four-failure count; PlatformIO's reader additionally mislabeled that
positive exit status as SIGILL. This was not an observed hardware fault or
native signal crash. Those red results are not reinterpreted as passes.

#### Implemented production boundary

- New `lib/node_event_delivery/src/node_event_creation.h` supplies a bounded
  `TrackedCreationSink` adapter shared by all existing local producers. It
  delegates the original durable enqueue and samples the clock **after**
  successful commit/read-back. Only ENQUEUED invokes controller tracking for
  that returned slot; rejection/failure does not initialize or refresh timing.
- `Controller::trackEnqueued()` in `node_event_delivery.h` forwards to the
  existing per-slot policy primitive. Only the newly occupied slot's volatile
  baseline and uncommitted milliseconds reset. No selection, attempt,
  checkpoint, FIFO, deadline, or durable mutation is added by tracking.
  Successful tracking time is also observed by the existing clock diagnostic;
  the counter remains present, and backward inputs are not clamped or hidden.
- If tracking cannot be established, the controller degrades and the creation
  adapter returns STORAGE_FAILURE, not ENQUEUED. The committed Event and
  actual store enqueue result remain retained. Subsequent creation through
  the degraded adapter is blocked. Existing producer failure handling disables
  production Event work; no durable record is silently freed/discarded.
- `src-rx/main.cpp` binds that adapter to the existing store/controller and
  Arduino `millis()`. A pending Event DIO1 completion now uses
  `txCompletedNow()` from `node_event_service_time.h`, sampling after any
  producer commit/tracking in that loop, rather than reusing loop-start time.
  Otherwise a fresh enqueue baseline could be followed by an older completion
  policy input. `serviceNodeEvents()` already samples fresh service time after
  packet processing and remains unchanged. No unsigned-time clamp, watchdog,
  retry-policy change, or scheduling workaround was added.

Recovery/reboot debit, sixty-second checkpoints, powered-runtime lifetime,
Event identity allocation, attempts, correlation/admission, terminal
reclamation, persistence records, Wire Protocol, Host schemas/minors, UI
rendering/navigation, and firmware version metadata are unchanged. The
controller's empty-queue behavior is not repurposed as an enqueue boundary.

#### Validation and evidence

Tools: PlatformIO Core 6.1.19, Python 3.14.6, existing pyserial 3.5; native
Unity tests and existing compile fixtures. No Host utility command targeting
a device was sent. Host Python tests used their normal non-physical fixtures.

| `pio test -e native -f test_node_event_delivery` | **48/48 PASS** |
| `pio test -e native` | **823/823 PASS**, all 35 native programs, zero failures/skips |

cases alone, followed by the complete delivery suite and full native gate.

before compilation; rerunning with approved existing-cache access passed.

The four formerly failing cases now retain the full 3,600 seconds, QUEUED
state, zero attempts and zero reversals at B's first service, then reach READY
and arm B's own first attempt. The explicit tracking control remains passing.
Six additional delivery cases cover post-commit clock ordering and charging
enqueue-to-first-service time, a WAIT_ADMISSION head and queued followers with
independent checkpoint boundaries/FIFO, fail-closed post-enqueue tracking with
durable recovery/debit, rejected enqueue and invalid tracking, fresh completion
after the 224677 -> 224678 ms enqueue boundary, and legitimate uint32 wrap.
Existing reboot/checkpoint, retry, reclamation and bad-caller negative controls
remain passing; no test assertion was relaxed to conceal reversed time.

The two new Python wiring checks require both producer paths to use the
tracked sink/failure gate and require the Event DIO1 branch to sample fresh
completion time. The ESP32 storage fixture also instantiates the same adapter
with the actual Arduino `millis()` type. These are shared-component, compile,
and source-wiring checks, **not** execution of the complete production main
loop against physical hardware.

#### Validation-environment deviation: local build outputs were not preserved

At 13:51-05:00, the post-test artifact check found both `.pio/build/tx/` and
`.pio/build/rx/` absent. Installed PlatformIO source inspection identified its
automatic cleanup mechanism: `compute_project_checksum()` includes the C/C++
file-name list; `clean_build_dir()` recursively removes the shared build tree
when that signature changes. Adding the new header changed that list before
the first native build. The build tree's new checksum timestamp was 13:48.
This explains the lost local qualification outputs; no explicit cleanup or
TX/RX build command was issued. The agent should have isolated the native
build directory before adding a source file. Later fixture builds used
`--disable-auto-clean`. No replacement qualification artifacts were built.
No matching replacement binaries were found in the repository or accessible
temporary locations checked; recovery from an external operator archive has
not been established. Prior fingerprints remain historical evidence, not a
claim that the binaries still exist locally:

### 12.29 Independent full-cycle, saturation, retry and tombstone characterization

**Continuation of the operator-supplied series in 12.28.** These are
characterization observations, not retroactively authorized or completed

#### Full cycle and saturation: requests 0x194A–0x1950

From HOME the operator completed one full screen cycle back to HOME, waking
as necessary. Node schema-1 request **0x194A** reported:

- enqueueAccepted **11** (+5 from 0x1949), eventAttempts **11** (+5 at this
  snapshot), admissionsAcknowledged **8** (+2).
- Last producer Event **0x8B**; custody **3**; oldest current Event **0x89**,
  BACKOFF, attempts **3/5**, remaining lifetime **3594 seconds**.
- eventsExpired, queueFullRejected, persistenceFailures, storageCorruptions
  each **0**.

The five new identities are reported as 0x87–0x8B. Two admitted/released;
three remained in custody. Since the first eight admitted Events each used
one attempt, the aggregate 11 attempts are consistent with those eight plus
three attempts for FIFO head 0x89, not one attempt for every enqueued Event.
The later Hub schema-1 request **0x194B** reported capacity **8**, ACTIVE **8**,
CONSUMED **0**, successfulAdmissions **8**, recent admission **0x88**, and
hubCapacityRejections **13**. Derived EMPTY is **0**. These are sequential
live snapshots: RF processing continued between them; they are not one atomic
cross-board snapshot. In particular 13 rejections need not already have
occurred at the earlier 11-attempt Node snapshot.

**0x194C POLL_EVENTS** returned oldest ACTIVE identity
**`0x10:0x85032967:0x00000081`**, BUTTON / SHORT_PRESS.
**0x194D CONSUME_EVENT** consumed exactly that identity. The subsequent Hub
state was ACTIVE **7**, CONSUMED **1**, EMPTY **0**, occupied custody **8**.
This was ACTIVE->CONSUMED, not a deletion or an EMPTY-slot creation.

Five seconds after that consume, Node schema-1 **0x194E** reported:
enqueueAccepted **11**, eventAttempts **23**, admissionsAcknowledged **8**,
attemptsExhausted **3**, custody **0**, EMPTY, eventsExpired **0**.
Hub schema-1 **0x194F** reported ACTIVE **7**, CONSUMED **1**, derived EMPTY
**0**, successfulAdmissions **8**, hubCapacityRejections **13**,
hostConsumptions **1**, hostPollsReturnedEvent **1**, recent admission **0x88**.

Node schema-2 **0x1950** retained Event **0x8B**, attempt **5**, controller
**FAILED**, terminal_reclaimed **3**. The retained terminal observation is
not a claim that a FAILED record still occupies custody: schema 1 reports
zero custody and the reclamation counter accounts for the three terminal
records. Supplied aggregate lifecycle counters:

| Counter(s), each with the listed value | 0x1950 |
|---|---:|
| ready_selected, arbitration_granted | 23 |
| start_called, start_accepted, tx_entered | 23 |
| dio_event, dio_classified_event, tx_completed_called, wait_entered | 23 |
| ack_decoded, ack_matched | 21 |
| admission_timeout | 2 |
| backoff_entered, backoff_due, retry_ready | 12 |
| expired_queued, expired_ready, expired_prepare, expired_tx, expired_wait, expired_backoff | 0 |
| clock_reversed | 0 |
| terminal_reclaimed | 3 |

Aggregate reconciliation under the source FIFO/five-attempt policy:

- **11 enqueues = 8 admitted/released + 3 attempt-exhausted/reclaimed**.
- **23 attempts = 8 successful first attempts + 3 x 5 unsuccessful attempts**.
- From 0x194A to 0x194E, **+12 attempts = 2 remaining for 0x89 + 5 for 0x8A
  + 5 for 0x8B**. This is FIFO/counter-based reconstruction, not individual
  RF traces for each identity.
- **23 attempts = 21 matched ACKs + 2 admission timeouts**.
- **21 matched ACKs = 8 successful admissions + 13 Hub CAPACITY responses**.
  Matched ACKs include rejection statuses, not just ADMITTED.
- **12 backoffs = 3 exhausted Events x 4 retry intervals**. No lifetime
  expiry or sixth attempt is indicated.

All 23 accepted Event starts reached DIO1 observation, Event classification,
txCompleted and WAIT by the aggregate counters. This further weighs against
missing/misclassified DIO1 or a generally unserviced retry scheduler in this
runtime. No new watchdog or retry-policy change is supported by this evidence.

**Capacity timing boundary:** 0x194B already reports all 13 capacity rejections
later seen at 0x194F. Consuming 0x81 did not create EMPTY capacity, but it DID
make that older same-source proof eligible for normal replacement by a later
Event, including 0x89–0x8B if received while still eligible. Source
`Ledger::admit()` tries EMPTY first, then `oldestReclaimable()`; occupancy 8
alone is not CAPACITY when an eligible CONSUMED proof exists. Thus the record
does not establish rejection of any new post-consume arrival despite a
replaceable proof. The exact position of the last attempts/timeouts relative
to the consume is unobserved. The +12 attempts span the earlier 0x194A
snapshot through 0x194E, not necessarily the five seconds after CONSUME.
Full ACTIVE custody explains the observed CAPACITY responses; it is incorrect
to explain subsequent exhaustion solely by saying CONSUME frees no EMPTY slot.

**Bounded characterization PASS:** one new Event was admitted using the

identity support that normal production path. This is not a physical torn-write
or reboot-durability test, nor proof of each unreported ledger record's bytes.

Similarly, between the earlier Hub baseline 1 ACTIVE / 5 CONSUMED / 2 EMPTY

### 12.30 Reconciliation, stale-slot evidence boundary and formal Scenario-1 disposition

**Analysis following 12.28–12.29.** The following dispositions coexist; none
replaces the original snapshots or rewrites a failed attempt into a pass:

| Evidence set | Disposition |
|---|---|
| Historical Stage-16 attempts and repaired-candidate Stage-16 bench result | Prior FAIL chronology and final PASS in 10.18 preserved |
| Stage-17 Scenario-1 attempts through 12.27 | Historical FAIL / STOPPED preserved |
| Later independent physical producer-policy characterization | PASS for the observed wake/navigation/producing transitions |
| Later bounded retry, terminal reclaim and CONSUMED replacement characterization | Positive physical evidence / bounded PASS as described in 12.29; not whole matrix-scenario passes |
| Formal Stage-17 Scenario 1 | **STOPPED — one bounded formal completion rerun required; no formal PASS yet** |

#### Earlier no-enqueue observation

0x1941 directly demonstrates that a later awake RADIO short produces an Event
on this candidate. The other ordinary screens and full-cycle +5 result agree
with source policy. A systematic claim that RADIO shorts cannot enqueue is
contradicted by these later observations. The earlier unchanged 0x1933 snapshot
is NOT contradicted as a historical observation, and its cause is still
unresolved; the no-enqueue result has not been reproduced in this new series.
It is best classified as an **unresolved isolated qualification observation**,
not an established continuing delivery defect or proven operator error.

Source review found production evaluates the PRESS-time producer context and
paired semantic events before UI navigation. An eligible awake RADIO short
should call creation before DEVICE appears. HOME/EVENT DIAG and wake gestures
are non-producing. The corrected ABOUT/HOME distinction reduces future
procedure ambiguity but does not prove which context applied to the earlier
failed gesture. Do not retroactively assign it to a wake, wrong screen, stale
Host snapshot, RF loss, or stale-slot expiry without evidence.

The separate suppression-reporting defect remains: paired RELEASE clears
`gestureSuppressed_` before SHORT_PRESS can report suppression, allowing older
CREATED diagnostics to remain. That affects observation of suppressed input;
it is not established as the cause of the reported awake RADIO->DEVICE
no-enqueue result. No change to that code or its tests occurred here. The older
OLED 6/0x66 versus Host 5/0x65 discrepancy and Event 0x66 attribution also remain
unresolved; later Host-driven successes do not resolve those captures.

#### Stale-slot remediation: what is physically supported

The independent series demonstrates repeated fresh creation after prior
releases, acknowledged admission/release, retry/backoff, three terminal
reclamations followed by another successful creation, and no reported premature
expiry. Source lowest-FREE-slot selection makes repeated physical slot reuse
consistent with these empty-Node intervals; physical slot indexes were not
reported. All six lifecycle expiry counters were zero at 0x1950; summary
eventsExpired remained zero at 0x1951, and clock_reversed was zero at both
0x1950 and 0x1953. The waiting head had **3594 seconds** remaining at 0x194A,
consistent with a fresh **3600-second** budget aging normally over a short
interval, not an inherited already-exhausted budget. Later failures were
five-attempt exhaustion, not lifetime expiry.

This is **sufficient bounded physical support for fresh enqueue/slot reuse in
the Hub-present gate**, alongside the deterministic remediation regressions
already recorded in 12.9. It removes the earlier situation in which no new
enqueue existed to exercise the repair at all. It is NOT a measured full
3600-second lifetime run, a precisely timed assertion of the initial remaining
budget, or physical reproduction of the greater-than-one-lifetime empty idle
interval. No exact enqueue/previous-slot timestamps, checkpoint/reboot-debit
or torn-write experiment was supplied here. The native long/ordinary-idle
regressions remain the exact boundary proof; no hour-long physical wait is
added as a new Scenario-1 requirement. CONSUMED replacement is complementary
Hub evidence, not proof of Node per-slot clock initialization by itself.

#### Why formal Scenario 1 is not yet PASS

The normative implementation brief Section 23 requires a documented empty-store
baseline, one generated Event through QUEUED/TX/WAIT, durable Hub ACTIVE,
exact ACK before Node FREE, admission/acknowledgment increments, and **POLL
returning that identity**. The approved 11.5 baseline clarification permits
retained CONSUMED proofs while requiring Node custody 0 and Hub ACTIVE 0; it
does not require literally empty Hub storage.

| Normative evidence | Independent series coverage |
|---|---|
| Correct repaired candidate / board attribution | Supplied identity and artifact attribution in 12.28; prior exact flash record preserved |
| Baseline Node custody 0, Hub ACTIVE 0 with proofs retained | Node empty; independent baseline already had ACTIVE 0x81. The 12.25–12.26 no-drain delta workflow explicitly accepted this nonzero ACTIVE state for its diagnostic attempt, not a silent rewrite of Section 23's baseline |
| One controlled new Event | 0x1941: new 0x82, exactly +1 enqueue/attempt/acknowledged release; later multi-Event counters are not attributed exclusively to 0x82 |
| Delivery, exact ACK and release | Supported by 0x1941's summary counters and production counter semantics; aggregate lifecycle progression and identity-correlated 0x8C retry/release provide additional bounded evidence |
| Hub durable ACTIVE for the same generated Event | Aggregate admissions through 0x88; direct recent-admission correlation for 0x8C. No separately reported immediate Hub identity/ordinal snapshot for 0x82 |
| POLL returns the controlled new identity | **Not supplied.** The sole POLL 0x194C returned baseline 0x81, not new 0x82 or 0x8C |

Positive later observations may satisfy a qualification gate without repeating
an old diagnostic isolation plan; a prior FAIL alone does not require a ritual
rerun. Here, however, the only POLL covers the preexisting Event, and no later

### 13.2 Pre-stimulus procedure constraints — no mutation or power action yet

Source/authority inspection identified the following before risking the sole
Event. These are procedure constraints, not new firmware defects.

**Retained ACTIVE versus single final POLL.** The Section-23 Hub-absent/restored
row does not require a zero-ACTIVE Hub baseline. The operator permits retaining
0x8D and conditionally authorizes archival/consume only if the normative row
requires zero ACTIVE. However, Host Protocol 0.2 Section 13.2 and
`HostEventService::poll()` return only the oldest ACTIVE admission ordinal,
with no selector for a newer identity. If 0x8D remains ACTIVE, the required
single final POLL must return 0x8D even if the new Scenario-2 Event succeeds.

Therefore no consume was inferred from the conditional authorization. The
smallest proposed clarification is explicit permission to POLL/archive the
currently retained 0x8D, CONSUME exactly that returned identity once, and verify
Hub 0 ACTIVE / 8 CONSUMED / 0 EMPTY before power removal. This preserves its
canonical evidence/proof and allows the single final POLL to return the new
Event; it is needed for the requested Host verification, not because Section
23 independently demands empty storage. No POLL or CONSUME has been performed
in Scenario-2 preparation. If 0x8D must remain ACTIVE, the final-POLL acceptance
procedure must instead be explicitly revised; no firmware/protocol change is
proposed.

**Bounded absence and restoration timing.** Production
`ADMISSION_TIMEOUT_MILLISECONDS=2500` starts at Event TX completion; four
backoffs are 1000/2000/4000/8000 ms plus 0..250 ms jitter each, with five
committed attempts maximum. With the previously observed approximately 233 ms
TX interval and prompt scheduling, exhaustion can occur around **28.665–29.665
seconds after first TX start** (5 x [233+2500] + 15000 + jitter). This is a
source/timing estimate, not a promised physical deadline: radio arbitration,
startup and actual TX timing matter. It is not safe to spend this interval on
ordinary chat replies, per-command approval delays and then Hub boot.

Before stimulus, a locally timed capture/restoration workflow must be arranged
with the operator ready to restore Hub power well before exhaustion and with
Node-only absent-state requests ready to run at a safe observed/timed radio
phase. The first timeout/backoff, retained identity/attempt/lifetime, and
hub_unavailable=true must be evidenced while Hub is off, not reconstructed
only after restoration. Node Host diagnostics are logically read-only but
production Host handling can interrupt Event TX; captured lifecycle must
exclude/identify host_during_tx rather than assume a timed sample is safe.
No helper, timing fixture, new fault-injection mechanism, deadline extension,
or retry change was implemented. No Event will be requested before this
coordination is resolved; if exhaustion occurs, STOP, never a second stimulus.

The least destructive planned absence method is removal/restoration of **all
Hub power**, preserving NVS, with Node power and both antennas untouched.
USB disappearance alone proves disconnection of the Host interface, not loss
of RF power if a battery/other source remains. Actual Hub power sources,
including any battery, must be confirmed before selecting the exact physical
disconnect action, as already required in 11.1. No antenna removal, Node
reset, serial reset command or unverified USB-only power-off is proposed.

**Reboot-relative Hub diagnostics.** The intended Hub power cycle resets its
volatile runtime counters; the reliability design explicitly distinguishes
them from durable ledger records. `src-tx/main.cpp::setup()` recovers the
ledger before radio readiness and restores receive at setup completion.
The observed pre-off successfulAdmissions=10 will NOT be expected to become
11 across a cold boot: one new admission means **+1 within the restored boot**
(normally reported count 1), correlated with persistent ACTIVE/identity and
ordinal evidence. That boot's zero counter initialization is source-known,
not necessarily a separately observed pre-admission Host snapshot. Node
counters remain continuous because Node is not reset. Hub ordinal reservation
uses blocks of 32; a forward ordinal gap after recovery is expected and must
not be misclassified as Event loss or require ordinal 43. Actual new ordinal
must be recorded. No runtime count or ordinal rule is being changed.

#### Staged operator-local procedure — all actions below NOT EXECUTED

After separate approval, launch the exact command documented in the helper
README in a direct local Dreadnought terminal. No chat approval or tool approval
may be needed inside the timed window. Existing terminal/Python are available;
no sudo is designed into the procedure. If device permissions or terminal
access are inadequate, stop before stimulus rather than modify the system.

Before disconnecting, the helper requires readiness confirmation that antennas
remain attached, Hub USB is its **sole** power source (no battery/other supply),
and manual reconnection/Enter by T+10 is comfortably achievable. The last power
condition is not established by software or historical tty presence. If
uncertain, do not arm. It then checks exact serial/VID:PID/cdc_acm/by-id identity
and Node summary/lifecycle plus Hub summary against archived baseline logs.

At the prompted action only, the operator disconnects Hub USB power, leaving
Node powered and antennas untouched, and confirms Enter. Helper verifies Hub
USB identity and tty disappearance. Operator then wakes/navigates Node
non-producing to visibly awake RADIO and confirms Enter. Node summary/lifecycle
are checked again before arming. Ordinary display timeout/wake is allowed;
OLED provides navigation only, not lifecycle evidence. No photographs or Event
screen readings are required.

| Time relative to operator stimulus confirmation | Staged action |
|---|---|
| T+0 | One awake RADIO short press/release -> DEVICE, immediately followed by local Enter within 1 second of release. No second GPIO press. |
| T+0 to T+4.5 | Host silent. |
| T+4.5 | Node schema 1 then schema 2; each CLI timeout 0.5 s / process budget 0.75 s. No retries. |
| Immediately after those reads; deadline T+6.25 | Print prominently `RESTORE HUB NOW`; also print on a failed absent capture and stop qualification. |
| By T+10 | Operator reconnects only Hub and immediately confirms Enter. |
| By T+12 | Exact Hub USB identity, cdc_acm and matching by-id link must reappear; accept changed tty number, reject wrong/ambiguous identity. |
| Next 3 seconds, ending by T+15 | Boot allowance without Host traffic; USB appearance/time allowance is not independent proof of RF readiness. |
| Until T+28 | Continue Host silence for natural later retries, including a late fifth attempt if necessary. Hub must already be restored, not left absent until this time. |
| T+28 onward | Node summary, Node lifecycle, Hub summary, exactly one final POLL, each only after preceding checks pass. No CONSUME. |

Planned fresh request IDs, all minor 2, baud 115200, raw hex/JSON, existing local
operation and identity-resolved by-id port:

| IDs | Requests, in order |
|---|---|
| 0x19A0 / 0x19A1 / 0x19A2 | Initial Node summary / lifecycle / Hub summary |
| 0x19A3 / 0x19A4 | Post-navigation pre-stimulus Node summary / lifecycle |
| 0x19A5 / 0x19A6 | Absent-Hub Node summary / lifecycle |
| 0x19A7 / 0x19A8 / 0x19A9 / 0x19AA | Restored Node summary / lifecycle / Hub summary / sole POLL |

Initial/arming/post commands use 1 second CLI / 1.5 seconds process budgets.
Every command/result/exit and operator confirmation is wall/monotonic timestamped
into a unique capture directory with raw encoded/decoded TX/RX and JSON. The
physical release-to-Enter lag is operator-attested, not directly measured.

#### Timing margin, acceptance and limitations

Production policy remains unchanged: 2.5-second admission timeout, 1/2/4/8-second
backoffs plus 0..250 ms jitter. With the previously observed ~233 ms Event TX,
idealized absent-Hub fifth-attempt exhaustion is about 28.7..29.7 seconds after
first TX start. Reconnection by T+10 and USB readiness by T+12 provide roughly
17 seconds of nominal margin, approximately 16 seconds allowing a one-second
confirmation lag. The three-second boot allowance leaves later normal retry
opportunities. No sleep was added to firmware or transport; waits exist only
in this temporary qualification coordinator.

This margin is conditional, **not a hard-real-time guarantee** of human action,
Linux scheduling, USB enumeration, firmware boot or RF readiness. Offline tests
cannot guarantee hardware boot duration. If the physical turnaround cannot
comfortably fit, do not arm. Missed deadlines stop without another Event or
automatic request; promptly restoring Hub power after a failed capture is safe
recovery, not continuation or replacement passing evidence. A Host diagnostic
can affect radio ownership despite read-only Event semantics; absent capture
targets WAIT/BACKOFF, and any TX-interference evidence stops the run.

Checks require exactly one enqueue/new full identity, retained Node custody
while Hub absent, timeout/backoff/retry progression, fresh remaining lifetime,
and no expiry, reversal, persistence/degradation or contradictory counters.
Post-restoration must show the same identity on a later attempt, exact ADMITTED
ACK decode/match, normal RELEASED/zero custody, and no failed/expired terminal
reclamation. Hub must hold **1 ACTIVE / 7 CONSUMED / 0 EMPTY** for that same
identity, replacing only one proof through normal admission, and final POLL
must return the canonical BUTTON/SHORT_PRESS Event. The new identity is read,
not predicted. Its actual admission ordinal is recorded; reboot allocation
reservation can cause a gap, so previous ordinal +1 is not required.

Restored Hub counters are interpreted only within the new boot: expect exactly
one new successfulAdmission there, **not pre-off 10 -> 11**. Persistent ledger
counts, identity and actual ordinal provide cross-boot correlation. Final POLL
does not consume the new Event. No Scenario 3 or Stage 18 action is included.

### 14.3 Scenario 14 characterization break and repair

An interleaved structured Host `PING` timed out after approximately 2.545
seconds while Node Event traffic was active. Qualification stopped and the
owning path was characterized before continuing.

Source inspection established that production Hub structured COMMAND traffic
did not participate in the existing `HubArbiter` ownership state even though
the arbiter already modeled `IDLE_RECEIVE`, `COMMAND_TX`,
`COMMAND_WAIT_ACK`, `COMMAND_WAIT_RESPONSE`, and `EVENT_ACK_TX`. The narrow
repair integrated the existing ownership model at production boundaries:

- structured start acquires `COMMAND_TX` and restores the prior owner/deadline
  on synchronous start failure;
- TX completion advances to `COMMAND_WAIT_ACK`;
- a successful structured ACK advances to `COMMAND_WAIT_RESPONSE`;
- structured completion/release restores `IDLE_RECEIVE`; and
- Event-ACK preemption/restoration continues through the existing arbiter.

No command scheduling, ACK timing, Event retry/admission, or Wire Protocol
semantics changed. A rebuilt Hub artifact was flashed and the physical rerun
completed the remote `PING` in 0.716341 seconds while fresh Event traffic and
Hub-capacity rejection were occurring. **Scenario 14 final disposition: PASS.**

### 14.4 Final legacy regressions

Scenario 16 exercised this operation set once under Host Protocol minor 1 and
once under minor 2:

| Operation | Expected/observed result |
|---|---|
| `PING` | `OK` |
| `GET_DEVICE_INFO` | `OK` |
| `GET_STATUS` | `OK` |
| `GET_CAPABILITIES` | `OK` |
| `DESCRIBE_CAPABILITY 0x0101` | `OK` |
| `READ_CAPABILITY 0x0201` | `UNAUTHORIZED` |
| `SET_INDICATOR 0x0101 true` | `UNAUTHORIZED` |
| `RUN_PROCEDURE 0x0001` | `UNAUTHORIZED` |
| `GET_DIAGNOSTICS` | `OK` |

All 18 expected results passed. `0x0001` was only a structurally valid target
for the denial test; this evidence does **not** claim that procedure `0x0001`
is registered.

Scenario 17 used one authoritative Hub-only measurement:

| Counter | Baseline | After Host-absent interval | Delta |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| transmissions completed | 449 | 453 | +4 |
| decoded packets received | 467 | 471 | +4 |
| successful transactions | 449 | 453 | +4 |
| retries | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| acknowledgment timeouts | 0 | 0 | 0 |

This proves 4/4 autonomous legacy TEST/ACK transactions with zero retries and
zero ACK timeouts. An earlier cross-device sample showed Hub +3 and Node +4
because the helper sampled Hub first and transaction 4 completed between the
two reads. That difference is a measurement-boundary race, not a firmware
failure, and is not substituted for the authoritative Hub-only result.

### 14.6 Qualification-discovered repairs

The completed physical campaign found and cleared two release-blocking
production integration defects:

1. A newly enqueued Node Event could inherit volatile lifetime timing from a
   prior occupant of the same physical slot. The repair durably enqueues first,
   samples a fresh clock, and initializes only that new occupant through
   `trackEnqueued()`, preserving other queued Events and reboot debit/checkpoint
   semantics. Physical evidence subsequently showed repeated slot reuse,
   admission/release, backoff/retry, zero expiry increments, and zero clock
   reversals without stale-baseline immediate expiry.
2. Hub structured COMMAND traffic was not participating in the existing radio
   arbiter. The repair described in 14.3 restored explicit COMMAND ownership
   transitions and receive restoration, then passed the interleaved physical
   rerun.

Terminal FAILED/EXPIRED reclamation and the Linux atomic DTR/RTS Host-open
adapter discovered earlier in the campaign remain part of the repaired release
implementation and their full chronology remains preserved above.

### 14.7 Overall physical disposition

**Stage 16 — Bench Integration Gate: PASS on the repaired candidate.**

**Stage 17 — Physical Qualification: PASS with explicit physical-fixture
limitations for Scenarios 3, 6, and 10.** All other normative scenarios passed,
including the repaired Scenario 14 rerun. The limitations are not represented
as physical PASS results.

Physical qualification used pre-promotion firmware that intentionally reported
`v0.6.0`, following the release precedent. Stage 18 promotes only the release
metadata to `v0.7.0`, reruns the complete software/build gate, and fingerprints
new release binaries. Those release hashes must not be described as the
physically flashed hashes merely because the behavior-bearing source is the
qualified source.

Wire Protocol 1 remains unauthenticated and unencrypted. F-02, F-03, and F-04
remain open. This qualification does not claim RF peer authentication,
confidentiality, authenticated persistent replay protection, RF jamming
resistance, multi-Node behavior, or life-safety suitability.

### 14.8 Stage-18 metadata promotion and release artifacts

Stage 18 promoted only the firmware release identity from `v0.6.0` to
`v0.7.0`; Wire Protocol remains 1, Host Protocol remains developmental 0.2
with minor-1 compatibility, Configuration Schema remains 1, and the hardware
profile remains `HELTEC_V4`. Version-sensitive native expectations were updated
to the promoted metadata.

The complete post-promotion release gate passed:

- native **823/823**;
- Python Host reference **36/36**;
- production wiring **11/11**, including the Stage-18 direct assertion for Hub
  structured-command arbiter ownership calls;
- both valid configuration fixtures;
- all three expected-negative configuration fixtures with their exact required
  diagnostics;
- both Host transport compile fixtures;
- the ESP32 Event storage compile fixture;
- Python syntax compilation;
- Hub/TX and Node/RX production builds; and
- `git diff --check`.

The final promoted release builds are:

| Role | RAM bytes | Flash bytes | `firmware.bin` bytes | SHA-256 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Hub / TX | 26,308 | 477,458 | 477,856 | `96d531473389230b7d7daf735f5a153f8cfaf10cef5f2fe0870e399b67482d84` |
| Node / RX | 27,124 | 485,710 | 486,112 | `254def0448d2ef80a3b736158cd435c8039e9b2e2a79f4e0b9fc969b93b375f6` |

Copies were preserved under the ignored local directory
`.pio/qualification-candidates/v0.7.0-release-20260906/` and remeasured against
the ordinary `.pio/build` outputs. These are release artifact fingerprints,
not deterministic source identifiers. They were not flashed for another
physical run. The pre-promotion physical provenance in 14.1 and 14.5 remains
the authority for physical execution; the post-promotion gate establishes that
the version-promoted source and both production environments build and pass the
complete software regression.
